Deep analysis · four products kept separate, then compared

CADCraft vs Civil 3D vs BricsCAD Ultimate, and the case for Genesis

A source-checked comparison of three products and one plan: CADCraft, the open-source Rust CAD in this repository; Autodesk Civil 3D, the land-development benchmark; BricsCAD Ultimate, the perpetual-licence alternative from Octave (the name Bricsys took in 2026); and Genesis (working name), the simpler site-design CAD we intend to build on CADCraft. Genesis has five goals: simpler; better referencing and data connections; self-contained projects; faster references; zero crashes. Its scope adds old-school building techniques and new ones such as solar and rain collection as first-class objects.

Prepared 2026-10-21 in the Sparkey333/cadcraft_1 repository (CADCraft 0.10.0). Pure HTML and CSS, no scripts; prints cleanly. Prices are quoted in the currency of the page they came from (US dollars for Autodesk and the market table, euros for the Octave store; no official US-dollar BricsCAD list price was found), as indexed on the date above; verify before buying.

Executive summary

1 · CADCraft

Open source · pure Rust · 2D drafting · free

The code we own. Pre-alpha AutoCAD look-alike with unusual strengths (agent control, never-crash engineering, five platforms) and no civil features yet.

2 · Autodesk Civil 3D

Subscription · Windows only · the industry default

The benchmark for land development: surfaces, alignments, corridors, grading, pipes, parcels, plan production. Powerful, heavy, expensive, and the product whose users complain most about crashes and broken references.

3 · BricsCAD Ultimate

Octave, the name Bricsys took in 2026 when Hexagon spun it off · perpetual licence available · Windows, Linux, macOS · V27 launched the day before this page

The strongest AutoCAD-compatible alternative with a perpetual option, 3D, BIM and mechanical in one box, and a young set of civil tools.

4 · Genesis (working name)

To be built · open source · land development and site design

Simpler than Civil 3D, more complete for sites than BricsCAD, built on CADCraft's engine and never-crash discipline, with data connections and sustainable-building techniques designed in from the start.

Numbered in the order the page discusses them, not ranked.

Verdict in one paragraph. Civil 3D remains the only one of the three shipping products that does the whole land-development job, and it is also the one whose users complain most about crashes, bloat, reference breakage and price. BricsCAD Ultimate is the best value for AutoCAD-style drafting with a perpetual licence and credible 3D and BIM, but its civil tools stop well short of a Civil 3D replacement. CADCraft is not yet a tool anyone should run a project on (pre-alpha, lossy DWG save, no civil objects), yet it is the right foundation for Genesis because of what it already has that the other two cannot offer: an open, crash-guarded Rust engine, a command model that agents and scripts drive identically, a browser build, and a licence that lets us ship. Genesis would therefore be built as a new product that depends on CADCraft's library crates and forks its engine and UI, not as a fork that chases AutoCAD menu parity.

Prices at a glance

Civil 3D, annual named-user subscription: about USD 2,870–3,175 per year. Autodesk's compare page (an undated snapshot) says 2,870 and its product page 'from 240 a month'; resellers list 2027 seats at 3,167–3,175 after a 2026 list increase; the current Autodesk list price was not confirmed. The AEC Collection is USD 3,675 per year on autodesk.com's 2027 page. BricsCAD Ultimate: EUR 2,640 one-time perpetual licence including the first year of maintenance, then an optional EUR 440 a year, or EUR 1,176 a year by subscription, on the Octave store; no official US-dollar list price was found. CADCraft: free. Sources and dates are in each product's identity card and in Appendix A.

What this means for the plan

  • Keep CADCraft moving as its own project (section 1); do not bend it toward civil work. Genesis depends on CADCraft's geom, dxf, dwg, color, doc, fonts, render, constraints, io and mcp crates as published libraries, and forks engine and ui-egui to prune the AutoCAD surface and replace the chrome.
  • Treat Civil 3D (section 2) as the capability benchmark and the complaint list: every high-severity complaint in the pooled review tables is a design requirement for Genesis.
  • Treat BricsCAD (section 3) as the commercial-model benchmark: perpetual-plus-maintenance pricing and cross-platform support are what buyers reward.
  • Genesis (section 4) would win on five things, in this order: never crashing, one self-contained project file with live but repairable links, a feature set scoped to sites rather than highways, open data connections (terrain, soils, flood, rainfall, solar), and a price small firms and owner-builders can say yes to.

Method and sources

Four kinds of evidence were used, and each table says which one it rests on.

  • Measured in this repository (CADCraft): the source on branch claude/sweet-pasteur-e6o9dg at 0.10.0, the project's own parity documents, a release build, a headless smoke test and a 200,000-entity benchmark, all on 2026-10-21, plus the upstream issue tracker.
  • Vendor documentation and stores (Civil 3D, BricsCAD): Autodesk and Octave (Bricsys) product pages, help centres, release notes and price pages, as indexed by search engines and the Autodesk help index on 2026-10-21. Direct page fetches were blocked for the research, so vendor text comes from search-engine captures, and Civil 3D's standalone list prices are older snapshots marked as such.
  • User reviews and forums: G2, Capterra, GetApp, Software Advice, TrustRadius, SourceForge, Trustpilot, the Autodesk Community, RPLS, AUGI and Bricsys forums, GitHub issue trackers and trade blogs, 2024–2026. Reddit, Hacker News and YouTube were unreachable and contribute nothing to this document. Reviews are paraphrased; counts and star ratings are quoted with their URL and date.
  • Domain references for land development, old-school building and sustainable techniques: public agencies (USGS, NRCS, FEMA, NOAA, NREL, EPA), standards bodies (LandXML, OGC, EPSG) and practitioner literature.

The material was gathered by nine parallel research passes, one per topic (9 returned), each writing notes with a URL for every item. The passes then nominated 119 key claims (prices, feature presence, ratings, counts); independent fact-checkers, told to refute them, examined 54 of those so far and overturned 4. The corrections are in Appendix A, and anything a checker could not confirm is marked unverified where it appears. The full notes are committed next to this page under docs/genesis/research/.

Limits of this research. Web pages were read through a proxy that refused many hosts, so several review-site figures come from search-engine snippets rather than opened pages; each research pass also had a bounded search budget. Items that rest on a snippet say so. Civil 3D's and BricsCAD's inventories describe the versions current on the research date (2027.1 and V26, with V27's launch additions listed separately).

Clean-room note. No Autodesk or Octave software was run, decompiled or copied for this document; AutoCAD, Civil 3D and BricsCAD are described from their public documentation and from users. Product names are used nominatively; see Appendix C.

1CADCraft (open source): the starting point

CADCraft is the open-source starting point: a clean-room, pure-Rust 2D drafting application that imitates AutoCAD's command line, prompts, snaps, layers, dimensions, hatches, blocks, layouts and DXF/DWG files, built from scratch in 2026 by the ArtCraft team, mostly by AI agents working under a strict never-crash and clean-room protocol. This fork (Sparkey333/cadcraft_1) is a snapshot of upstream storytold/cadcraft at 0.10.0. Everything in this section was measured in the repository or run on 2026-10-21; nothing here is marketing.

Identity card

Version
0.10.0 (tagged 2026-10-20, published on the upstream Releases tab 2026-10-21; eight releases between v0.1.0 on 2026-10-08 and v0.10.0)
Licence and price
MIT OR Apache-2.0; free, no account, no telemetry
Language and size
Pure Rust: 193,684 lines in 618 files including tests, across 12 library crates, 3 apps and xtask (counted 2026-10-21 in this fork; the architecture doc's 57,000 is from 2026-10-10)
Commands
431 engine command ids (413 written out in the source plus 18 macro-generated constraint commands; the parity checklist of 2026-10-16 says 427 and the architecture doc's 295 is from 2026-10-10), 240 of them with interactive prompt machines, plus 56 UI-only commands; cadcraft-cli commands prints the catalogue
AutoCAD menu breadth
245 of 491 AutoCAD 2027 for Mac menu items have a live command (50%) per ROADMAP.md, re-measured 2026-10-10; the checklist regenerated on 2026-10-16 counts 283 (58%); 2D menus only ≈ 65%
Ready for real work
≈ 38% (project's own weighted estimate); mainstream 2D drafter ≈ 28%; essentials user ≈ 46%; stage pre-alpha
Platforms
macOS (universal), Windows x64 / x86 / arm64 (signed MSI and portable zip), Linux x86_64 / aarch64 / riscv64 (AppImage, Flatpak, deb, rpm), FreeBSD, web (WebAssembly)
Interface languages
16: English plus Arabic, Czech, German, Greek, Spanish, French, Hungarian, Italian, Korean, Brazilian Portuguese, Russian, Swedish, Turkish, Ukrainian and Simplified Chinese (1,010–1,360 catalogue rows each, mostly AI-assisted, native proofreading pending); engine messages English only
File formats
DXF read R12–2018, ASCII and binary, write 2000–2018; DWG R13–2018 read and 2000–2018 write through the MPL-2.0 acadrust crate (native builds only); PDF plot, SVG and PNG export; SVG import; templates (.dwt); linetype (.lin), hatch-pattern (.pat) and alias (.pgp) text files. Plot style tables are CADCraft's own JSON format behind the familiar .ctb/.stb names; vendor .ctb/.stb/.pc3 binaries are not read
Agent control
MCP server (headless or bridged to the running app), JSON control channel, headless CLI (info, convert, run, commands, perf, sample, mcp), AutoLISP core (5,620-line interpreter), .scr scripts
Crash safety
unsafe forbidden; 15 crate roots deny unwrap/expect/panic; 21 catch_unwind call sites (Session::execute and the LISP runner restore the drawing from a snapshot; the others turn an escaped panic into an error or a logged fallback); 100 test functions named hostile across the workspace (39 in the engine crate) among 2,110 #[test] functions under crates/; caps on every input-sized allocation; autosave with instance locks; AUDIT/RECOVER. Gap: no CI runs the test suite on pull requests
Built and run as part of this analysis. cargo build --release produced cadcraft-cli (3 min 08 s) and the desktop app cadcraft (6 min 18 s) on a 4-core Linux container with Rust 1.97. A headless script drew a line, a circle and a linear dimension, saved DXF, exported PNG; a sample floor plan converted DXF → DWG → SVG; the desktop app started under a virtual X display with a software Vulkan rasteriser, opened a control port, drew a circle and a polyline sent as JSON, zoomed to extents and wrote a 1600×1000 screenshot on request. See the verification log.

The alpha gate (precise drafting with tracking, blocks, plotting, lossless DWG save) is still failing on four of five workflows.

cadcraft-cli perf 200000 on 2026-10-21, 4 vCPU, no GPU, synthetic drawing. Real files behave differently: upstream users report a 47.8 MB DWG (985,000 entities) taking 20–28 s to appear and 4.5–5.1 GB of memory (issue #1076), a 76.8 MB xref freezing the app at 8.4 GB (#1625), and a 10 MB DWG failing after about two minutes (#57). The synthetic numbers show what the engine can do; the field numbers show what the DWG bridge and the memory model cost.

Referencing, data connections, file containment and crash safety (what Genesis would inherit)

These are the five goals set for the new app, so they were read in the source rather than taken from the docs. Each finding says what to keep and what to change; codes such as F23 or S1 in the evidence column are row ids in the project's docs/gaps.md.

TopicWhat to keep, what to changeEvidence
Project containerKeep CADCraft's stored-path policy (craft-docpath: local or own-share only, refusals become statuses) and the bare-file-name-beside-host fallback - together they make a folder-shaped project relocatable. Change: add a project manifest beside the drawings listing every reference (xref, data link, underlay, font, template, plot style) with project-relative path, content hash, size, mtime and role; 'package/eTransmit' becomes copy-by-manifest instead of AutoCAD's 40-70 h F23 feature. Persist xref status (CADCraft loses Unloaded) and the found-at path. Keep a .bak/previous version beside the atomic tmp+rename save, and make WBLOCK prune definitions like the clipboard does.crates/engine/src/doc_path.rs; xref/load.rs candidates(); crates/doc/src/tables.rs xref_status serde(default); cmd/blocks.rs run_wblock; docs/gaps.md F23
Link health checksKeep the per-reference status enum with a human reason (Loaded/Unloaded/Not Found/Unresolved(reason)/Unreferenced), computed at open and reload and coloured in the palette, and the rule that a bad reference never blocks opening. Change: add one linkcheck command that walks the manifest (xrefs, nested xrefs, data links, underlays) and reports found/missing/refused/changed-since-load using a hash or mtime+size recorded at load (CADCraft records neither); add a file watcher so a modified xref raises a reload notice (CADCraft has none); decide explicitly whether to canonicalise symlinks before the lexical docpath check.xref/load.rs Outcome/Missing; ui-egui/src/xrefs.rs status colouring; grep notify/watch in engine and ui: none; craft-docpath docs caveat on symlinks
Fast re-referencingKeep the copy-on-write EntityStore (Arc chunks of 1024, sharded handle index): snapshots, undo, autosave and spatial caches are pointer copies, and it is the natural substrate for sharing a loaded reference between host, palette and caches without copying. Change: CADCraft re-reads and re-imports the whole referenced file on every Reload and loads xrefs synchronously after OPEN; Genesis should keep a process-wide parsed-reference cache keyed by (canonical path, size, mtime, hash), load references on a worker like OPEN's read_file/finish_open split, give each reference its own R-tree so pick/snap through a reference is logarithmic, and consider viewport-extent demand loading for large site bases. Keep every cap (files, bytes, objects, depth 16, cycle chain).crates/doc/src/store.rs; crates/engine/src/spatial.rs; cmd/file.rs read_file/finish_open; xref.rs after_open; load.rs caps
Deterministic copy between drawingsKeep the merge rules verbatim: existing named definitions in the target win, anonymous * blocks get fresh generated names, nested blocks and record-to-record dependencies (layer->linetype, dimstyle->textstyle) travel with the objects, new handles, source drawing uid tracked for Paste-to-original. CADCraft implements this three times (clipboard, insert-from-file, import); Genesis should have one merge_definitions used by paste, insert, import, bind and packaging. Change: add a stable per-object GUID so re-pasting the same source object is an idempotent update, return a merge report (kept/renamed/dropped) as the command result for users and agents, and remap handles to preserve dimension associativity when both ends were copied (CADCraft always drops it). Keep DXF text as the cross-process clipboard format.cmd/clipboard.rs defs_for/merge/block_names/adopt; cmd/blocks/insert_file.rs; cmd/exchange.rs import_drawing
Crash-proofingKeep everything: deny lints for unwrap/expect/panic plus forbid(unsafe), Result everywhere, catch_unwind at every boundary (command, interactive step, prompt/preview, open, file read, DWG bridge, LISP, render thread) with restore from the pre-command Arc<Drawing>, non-finite refusal, the hostile_* test convention plus a cross-command hostile_params_never_panic, caps on every input-sized loop, background autosave with lock files and tmp+rename, immediate autosave on GPU fault, GPU errors captured by callbacks. Add what CADCraft lacks: a CI gate running the full check on every PR (its S1 gap is why it self-scores stability 35% despite the architecture), an append-only command journal per drawing so recovery replays to the last action rather than the last 10-minute autosave, web autosave (IndexedDB), a DWG path that does not inflate 6x into DXF text (#57 was the first crash-adjacent failure users reported; the upstream tracker now holds about 42 issues matching 'crash', including GPU startup failures and an xref re-path crash) (reconciled after the fact-check and critique, 2026-10-21), reader fuzzing, and opt-in crash/hang telemetry so 'zero crashes' is measurable.CLAUDE.md Never crash; crates/engine/src/guard.rs, lib.rs, finite.rs, autosave.rs; crates/dwg/src/lib.rs; docs/gaps.md S1, row 5, FF6; github.com/storytold/cadcraft/issues/57

Worst: what the field reports (upstream issues, 2026)

ThemeWhat the issues saySev.Source
CADCraft performance and memory on real DWGs and xrefs#1076: 47.8 MB DWG (985k entities) takes ~19.6 s headless, 22-28 s to appear, peaks 4.5-5.1 GB on an M1 Pro; intermediate DXF text 5.8x the input is built fully in memory. #1625 (0.8.0): a 76.8 MB xref attached to a 39.9 MB plan freezes the window 34 of 90 s and peaks at 8.4 GB; no human reply. #752: 'even mouse movement is choppy', whole PC freezes; AutoCAD handles files 4x larger smoothly.highissues
CADCraft DWG fidelity: files other CAD refuse or audit-fail#1041 corpus (23 office DWGs, 1.63M entities, v0.6.2): ODA and ezdxf read all output, but BricsCAD V26 refused 14 of 23 CADCraft-saved DWGs (assoc constraints, dynamic-block evaluation graphs); lost draw order (~2,680), annotative context (~957), ~95% of DIMASSOC, ~half of FIELDs, 2,575 of 2,580 3DSOLIDs; ~410 AUDIT messages per 5-layout drawing. #604: AutoCAD 2027 round trip went from 0 to 6 AUDIT errors and AC1032 became AC1018. #907: BricsCAD RECOVER breaks both layouts. Also #1463 layouts merged, #1321 empty viewports/wrong fonts, #1316 viewport UCS dropped, #1812/#1813 Aspire crashes on import.highissues
CADCraft crashes and startup failures in the field42 issues match 'crash': black/unresponsive window on 0.7.0 with Intel Ivy Bridge GPU (#1724, dup #1824: 0.6.0 worked), Metal device-lost on older Macs (#1631), SIGILL on quit (#1536), crash on resize (#1527), Mali/ARM64 (#1454), Raspberry Pi 5 (#955); XREF crash after re-pathing a missing 22 MB xref on Windows 11 while 'another CAD program opened the same file without complaint' (#1636, critical, no human reply); DWGs fail to open on macOS 27 (#1421). README itself notes wgpu OpenGL can crash some AMD setups before the window appears.highissues
CADCraft silent data loss on concurrent save; lossy saves#540: a user reproduced silent data loss on 0.6.2 (Flatpak/Debian) when two instances saved the same DWG; asks for conflict warnings, external-modification detection, single instance per drawing. README: entities not yet modelled are dropped on save, SHX fonts not read. Contributor: autosave/recovery/per-instance locks exist; .bak backups, fsync-durable saves and 'who has it' are missing.highissues
CADCraft input model and UI vs AutoCAD habits#1001: clicks lost while the mouse is moving in TRIM/STRETCH/LINE/MOVE/COPY and window selection; users must stop before clicking (fix PR #1555 under review). #729: panels cannot dock, Layer Properties Manager floats over the canvas, no saved workspaces. #1134: constraint tools existed but were undiscoverable; Fix did not hold a point; visibility checkbox inverted. Also no interactive DIMSTYLE (#850), PLOT dialog missing (#429), right-click Enter (#1387), printing in non-inch units broken (#1828).mediumissues
CADCraft xref experience22 issues match xref: crash on re-path (#1636), 8.4 GB freeze (#1625), XCLIP boundary ignored (#1581), xref layer overrides lost on save (#886, fixed), LISP cannot identify xref blocks (#1716), REFEDIT/REFSET/REFCLOSE landed for blocks (#813); editing an xref in place is still open, raster references not listed (#1756).highissues
CADCraft feedback loop loses the reporterBug reports arrive via Discord and are relayed by a bot; reporters often have no GitHub account so replies never reach them (#752, #1636, #1824, #738); several maintainer replies are labelled as written by Claude Code on a contributor's behalf; Discussions are disabled; (the fact-check corrected an earlier note: the Releases tab does list eight releases, v0.1.0 to v0.10.0).mediumissues

What reporters wish for (wishes that cite CADCraft issues)

  • Open the office's real DWGs (2004-2018, xrefs, layouts, dynamic blocks, associative constraints) exactly, and save files that AutoCAD and BricsCAD open with zero AUDIT errors and no version downgrade (cadcraft #604, #1041, #907; LibreCAD #3064).
  • Never silently drop entities, xdata or draw order on save; warn before any lossy write and keep the original untouched (CADCraft README caveat; #540).
  • Stay smooth on 20-50 MB drawings and multi-xref plans: background loading, visibility culling, first paint under ~12 s, peak memory under ~2.5 GB (#1076, #1625, #738; 'AutoCAD handles files 4x larger').
  • Zero crashes plus real recovery: atomic fsync-durable saves, .bak files, recovery journal, single-instance-per-drawing locks, conflict detection, and AUDIT/RECOVER equivalents (#540, #752; LibreCAD 2.2.1.6 crash list; FreeCAD 362 crash issues).
  • Robust startup on any GPU with a software/fallback renderer when Metal/Vulkan/DX12/OpenGL fails (#1724, #1631, #955, README AMD note).
  • AutoCAD-compatible muscle memory: command aliases, right-click Enter, clicks registered while the mouse moves, familiar selection, DIMSTYLE and PLOT dialogs, dockable panels and saved workspaces (nanoCAD ease score; #1001, #729, #850, #429; LibreCAD #3162).
  • Proper external references: relative paths, re-path without crashing, clipping, in-place edit, overrides preserved, raster images listed, fast load (#1636, #1581, #813, #886, #1756, #1625).
  • Reliable plotting in any unit and paper size with a working preview (#1828, #1791; LibreCAD printing reviews).
  • Integrated, current help and a support channel where the reporter actually sees the answer (LibreCAD docs reviews, Capterra support 3.1/5; CADCraft Discord relay).
  • A public real-file test corpus with per-release round-trip reports and AUDIT counts (cadcraft #1041 offer; LibreCAD PR #2855 method).
Reading the two records together. The engine refuses panics and restores the drawing when one escapes, and that holds in the headless tests; in the field the failures come from the layers the guards do not cover: GPU drivers and window creation at startup, memory growth on 50–80 MB DWGs and xrefs (the DWG bridge builds a DXF text 5–6 times the file in memory), and two instances saving the same file. Genesis would inherit the guards and add process isolation for the GPU path, streaming file I/O and file locking (principles in section 4.1).

Unique strengths

  • Credible never-crash engineering: pure Rust with unsafe forbidden, panic-denying lints on 15 crate roots, 21 catch_unwind call sites (Session::execute and the LISP runner restore the drawing, the rest turn an escaped panic into an error; recounted 2026-10-21), 100 hostile-named test functions (39 in the engine crate; recounted 2026-10-21) plus a property-based numeric suite, caps on every input-sized structure, autosave with instance lock files, AUDIT/RECOVER that never guess content, crash-surviving rotated logs.
  • Agent-first automation no commercial CAD ships natively: MCP server (headless or bridged to the app), JSON control channel with UI introspection and screenshots, cadcraft-cli batch runs, and an 'everything is a command' registry whose JSON form never opens dialogs.
  • Broadest platform reach of the three apps compared: signed Windows x64/arm64/x86, notarized macOS universal, Linux (AppImage/Flatpak/deb/rpm, aarch64, riscv64), FreeBSD, and a browser build.
  • Free and open (MIT OR Apache-2.0), clean-room, no account; DXF foreign-data round trip (#528), vector PDF with real text/layers/hyperlinks, SVG import/export, own parametric solver with DOF reporting, 16 interface languages.
  • Swappable UI enforced by a layering gate; copy-on-write document with cheap undo; R-tree picking at 200k entities; extreme development velocity with honest, weighted self-assessment docs.

Notable weaknesses

  • Pre-alpha: 4 of 5 daily AutoCAD workflows fail the repo's own alpha gate; ready-for-real-work ≈ 38%, mainstream 2D drafter ≈ 28%.
  • DWG save is lossy through the third-party acadrust bridge, never systematically verified in AutoCAD by the project (community issue #604 reports one round trip with 6 AUDIT errors), no real-file corpus; a 10 MB DWG failed after ~2 minutes; DWG passes through an in-memory DXF (1.2 GB for an 83 MB file).
  • No 3D at all (2D Vec2 kernel, no UCS) and no civil/land-development capability of any kind (points/surfaces/contours/alignments/grading/parcels/GIS/geolocation/survey all absent).
  • No PDF/raster/DWF/DGN underlays or image display (ATTACH is a stub), no SHX fonts (text metrics differ), binary CTB/STB/PC3 unreadable, no physical printers, no sheet sets/eTransmit/fields/SPELL, no dynamic-block authoring or XCLIP, no plug-in API or CUI.
  • Stability self-rated 35% with no CI test gate on pull requests; ~400 open PRs and ~390 open issues on the upstream page (unverified); Windows/Linux lightly tested; web build lacks DWG and autosave.
  • Geometry on ellipses/splines is tessellated, tolerances are absolute, polyline offsets don't recompute bulges; untested at survey-scale coordinates.
  • Documentation drifts internally (lines of code 57k vs 194k; menu breadth 245 vs 283; UI/UX 43% vs 48%; languages table lists existing catalogs as 'none'; hardware doc says no 3D mouse while code exists).

2Autodesk Civil 3D: the capability benchmark

Autodesk Civil 3D is AutoCAD plus a civil-engineering object model: dynamic surfaces, alignments, profiles, corridors, grading, parcels, pipe and pressure networks, survey and plan production, all linked so that a change to one updates the rest. It is the de-facto standard that reviewers, permitting agencies and clients expect in North America, and the product Genesis must be measured against for capability, not for size. Sold by subscription only, Windows only.

Identity card

Version
2027.1 (Aug 2026); 2027 (Apr 2026); 2026/2026.1/2026.2 (Apr–Dec 2025). DWG format AutoCAD 2018; AECC object format R2018.2 + versioned drainage objects.
Platforms
Windows 11 / Windows 10 (version 17763+), 64-bit x64 only (ARM unsupported); No macOS, Linux or web edition (AutoCAD Web cannot edit Civil 3D objects); Recommended: 3+ GHz CPU, 32 GB RAM (8 GB basic), 8 GB DirectX 12 GPU (2 GB DX11 basic), ~20 GB SSD; 16 GB+ RAM / 12 GB+ VRAM for point clouds (2025–2027 requirement pages; exact 2027 table unverified); Requires Autodesk sign-in + Desktop Connector for cloud, online maps, Assistant and drainage cloud analysis
Pricing

The research worker's summary, verbatim

Civil 3D is the de-facto standard, DWG-native, Windows-only, subscription-only land-development and transportation design suite built on AutoCAD + Map 3D. It covers the full chain (survey DB → surfaces → parcels/COGO → alignments/profiles with criteria-based design and superelevation → assemblies/corridors → grading + Grading Optimization → gravity/pressure pipes with integrated cloud drainage analysis → sections/QTO/mass haul → plan production/sheet sets) with a dynamic object model, country kits, Dynamo/.NET/COM automation and (2027) a Tech-Preview AI assistant. Its weakest areas for a 'Genesis'-style competitor are exactly the owner's goals: AECC objects are never backward compatible (no save-to-previous; proxies; LandXML is the only escape), data shortcuts/xrefs break when files move or permissions differ (Autodesk's 2027/2027.1 'Connected References' fix is gated behind the paid Forma Design Collaboration cloud tier), cloud collaboration is single-editor file-locking via Desktop Connector with unsupported areas, stability complaints are persistent (Autodesk's own 2027 fixed-issues list documents multiple crashes), it needs 32 GB RAM / 8 GB DX12 GPU, and it has no sustainability (solar, rainwater) tooling at all.

Unique strengths

  • Industry-standard breadth: one DWG-native product spans survey DB → surfaces → parcels/COGO → criteria-based alignments/profiles/superelevation → corridors → grading (+ Grading Optimization) → gravity/pressure pipes → integrated cloud drainage analysis → sections/QTO/mass haul → view frames/sheet sets, all dynamically linked so a change propagates everywhere
  • Agency fit: DOT and country kits (styles, templates, design criteria XML, reports), AASHTO superelevation tables, NCS layering, pay-item QTO with TransXML/FDOT formats
  • Automation depth: .NET and COM APIs (LISP/VBA reachable), Dynamo for Civil 3D with Player and 75+ drainage nodes, Subassembly Composer, Project Explorer template reporting, and (2027) an in-product AI assistant
  • Ecosystem: AEC Collection bundles InfraWorks, Revit, Navisworks Manage, ReCap Pro, Vehicle Tracking, Forma; IFC 4.3 extension; Map 3D GIS/FDO and ArcGIS connector built in
  • Large user/training base and hiring pool; extensive official help, tutorials, Autodesk University content; G2 reviewers rate it easier to learn than OpenRoads

Notable weaknesses

  • Referencing fragility: data shortcuts and xrefs break when files are moved/renamed, permissions differ, paths are long or Desktop Connector lags; Autodesk's own advice is 'never rename or move referenced files', and the 2027/2027.1 Connected References fix is gated behind the paid Forma Design Collaboration cloud tier
  • No backward compatibility for Civil objects: cannot 'save to previous' Civil 3D objects (proxies, commands disabled); LandXML or flattening are the only escape hatches, so firms must pin a release per project
  • Stability and performance: Autodesk's 2027 fixed-issues list itself documents many crashes; reviews on Capterra/G2/TrustRadius repeatedly cite crashes, slow large files and point clouds, and the need for a 32 GB / 8 GB-GPU workstation
  • Cost and licensing: subscription-only (~$2,870–3,175/yr standalone, $3,675/yr Collection, plus Forma Design Collaboration for cloud shortcuts), yearly list increases, Flex at ~$27/day; perpetual licences gone since 2016
  • Cloud collaboration is single-editor file locking via Desktop Connector with explicitly unsupported areas (survey DB, Sheet Set Manager, reference templates) and unlocked data-shortcut folder edits that 'may be lost'
  • Churn and gaps: SSA discontinued mid-transition (replacement is cloud-only), MrSID dropped in 2027, every plug-in must be rebuilt for .NET 10, Assistant/daylight tools are Tech Preview with 'results may not be accurate', legal-description writer is rigid, pressure networks have no LandXML path
  • Windows-only; no macOS, Linux or web editing; steep learning curve driven by the style/label/settings system
  • No sustainability or 'neo' tooling in-product: zero rainwater-harvesting, solar/PV, carbon or vernacular-construction content (those live in Autodesk Forma, a separate product)

What they wish existed

  • Stability first: ship releases when ready and fix decade-old bugs before adding AI, cloud or rebrands (forums, Capterra)
  • True multi-core / multi-threaded processing for all operations, not just regen/redraw (Idea: Multi-Core Support | Multi-Thread Processing, forums.autodesk.com/t5/civil-3d-ideas/multi-c…)
  • Skewed corridor sections/regions (Idea with 124 votes, open for ~a decade, forums.autodesk.com/t5/civil-3d-ideas/skewed-…)
  • Data references that are fast, survive move/rename/copy, and sync over VPN/ACC without minutes-to-hours stalls or hand repair
  • Save-back / cross-version compatibility for Civil 3D objects instead of proxies and LandXML round-trips
  • Lighter files: no RegApp/NOD bloat, reference-only surfaces by default, crash-safe saves
  • Production-ready grading: daylight feature line out of 'tech preview', reliable feature-line-to-surface, optimizer that honours ridge/drain lines
  • Pipe networks: vertical bends in pipe runs, runs without alignments, bulk edits, stable pressure networks and fittings that do not vanish (pressure piping was AUGI's #1 wish in 2011)
  • Labels that never disappear or drift; corridor labels auto-update; plan-view corridor and cross-slope labels (Ideas with ~20 votes each)
  • Native Mac / cross-platform version (Idea at Future Consideration)
  • Perpetual or fairer licensing, return of network licences, affordable seats for small firms, individuals and students, no auto-renew traps
  • Extensions, country/state kits and object enablers available at release; no silent removal of formats such as MrSID
  • Civil 3D object data exposed to AutoCAD Fields and OSNAP that works on all Civil 3D objects (AUGI 2011-2013 lists)
  • Better surveying workflows (least squares, point handling) so surveyors do not have to pair Civil 3D with Carlson
The review worker's summary, verbatim

Civil 3D is rated 4.4/5 on G2, Capterra, GetApp and Software Advice and 9.3-9.5/10 on TrustRadius: reviewers rate its feature depth highly (Capterra features 4.6) and call it the industry standard for surfaces, alignments, profiles, corridors and construction documents, but ease of use (4.0), support (3.7) and value (4.0) lag, and Autodesk the company scores 1.5/5 on Trustpilot over auto-renewals and support. The dominant negatives across every source class are crashes/fatal errors (2026 called "the most unstable AutoCAD-based release since R13" by one forum user; 2027 shipped a confirmed Save-Block-As freeze and dropped MrSID), slowness with large surfaces/corridors (single-threaded; feature-line edits of 10 s to >5 min on a 20 MB file), and data shortcuts that are path-fragile, break on move/rename/copy, and can take minutes to hours to sync over network/VPN/ACC. File bloat (RegApps, NOD objects, pasted corridors turning 20 MB into 1.6 GB) and ritual AUDIT/PURGE/RECOVER hygiene, style/template complexity, a months-long learning curve, no backward save of Civil 3D objects (proxy objects, version-locked enablers), subscription-only licensing ($2,870/yr list) and Windows-only round out the complaints. Top wishes: stability over new features, multi-core processing, skewed corridor regions (124 votes, open for a decade), resilient/fast references, save-back, lighter files, production-ready grading and pipe tools, labels that do not vanish, Mac support, fairer licensing. Access limits: Reddit is blocked for this search agent and direct page fetches were refused by the sandbox proxy, so Reddit and YouTube evidence is absent and ratings are as-indexed; Autodesk Community, RPLS, AUGI, DOT knowledge bases and review sites were used instead.

3BricsCAD Ultimate: the perpetual-licence benchmark

BricsCAD is a native-DWG CAD family sold in five editions that build on each other: Lite is 2D drafting with AutoLISP (LISP runs in every edition); Pro adds 3D modelling, the civil tools, point clouds and the BRX, .NET and COM developer APIs; Mechanical and BIM sit on Pro; Ultimate has everything. Its maker, Bricsys, became Octave in 2026 when Hexagon spun the business off, so vendor pages, review sites and release notes now carry either name. V27 launched on 20 October 2026, the day before this page was written; the inventory below was researched against V26 and lists V27 additions separately. BricsCAD is the AutoCAD alternative most firms actually migrate to, because commands, LISP routines and DWG files carry over. The civil toolset (civil points, TIN surfaces, alignments, grading, corridors, civil explorer) is real but is not a Civil 3D substitute. The perpetual licence, still sold in 2026, is the commercial model buyers keep asking Autodesk for.

Identity card

Version
V27 (launch keynote 20 October 2026; Windows release notes already at build 27.1.05) from Octave, the company Bricsys became in 2026 as a Hexagon spin-off. The inventory below was researched against V26 (October 2025, V26.2 March 2026) and marks V27 additions where known; V27 pricing was not re-checked (editor's own search, 2026-10-21)
Platforms
Windows 10 and 11, x86-64 only (Microsoft ended Windows 10 support 2025-10-14); Linux x86-64: Ubuntu 22.04 LTS and newer supported releases; openSUSE/Fedora builds after April 2022 with glibc >= 2.35; 3D acceleration unsupported on Intel graphics and dual-GPU laptops; macOS 12 Monterey or later; runs on Apple silicon through Rosetta 2 (since V21.2.06); native Apple-silicon build planned, not shipped; Hardware: Core i5/Ryzen 5 min (i7/i9, Ryzen 7/9 recommended); 8 GB RAM min, 16 GB recommended; 3 GB disk for Ultimate; No ARM, web or mobile build
Pricing
Ultimate perpetual (official store, EUR): EUR 2,640 one-time, includes first-year maintenance source
Ultimate perpetual in USD (third-party listing) (added from the editor's own web search on 2026-10-21): TrustRadius lists a one-time fee of USD 2,100 for Ultimate on premise (unofficial; its embedded data also shows 590, which is the Lite figure); the official store quotes EUR 2,640 only source
Ultimate maintenance renewal (official store, EUR): EUR 440 per year; 'from year two, renewal is up to you' (optional). Caveat: the reviews worker read Pro maintenance at EUR 710/yr on the same domain, which would make Ultimate cheaper than Pro; one of the two figures is a rendering or regional artefact, so confirm in the live store (reconciled after the fact-check and critique, 2026-10-21) source
Ultimate subscription (official store, EUR) (added from the editor's own web search on 2026-10-21): EUR 1,176 per year; EUR 3,171 for three years (regional Bricsys store pages; the US maintenance page also shows EUR 1,176/yr for Ultimate, which conflicts with the EUR 440 renewal figure on the product page: verify in the live store) source
Maintenance terms (official) (added from the editor's own web search on 2026-10-21): Every new licence includes one year of BricsCAD Maintenance; renewal is optional from year two and can be bought from 90 days before the contract ends source
Ultimate perpetual (UK reseller): GBP 2,257 (CAD Software Direct); GBP 1,890 incl. first-year maintenance (Brighter Graphics) - two reseller prices disagree source
Ultimate maintenance renewal (US resellers, USD): USD 484.00 (Novedge) vs USD 441 (acad2bcad); figures disagree, dates unverified source
Ultimate perpetual in USD (official US store): not found in this session (page fetch blocked); EUR 2,640 is roughly USD 2,800-3,100 by exchange rate, not a list price source
Perpetual availability 2026: Still offered per official store and resellers (page dates unverified); a forum thread criticises a new 'Upgrade and Support Policy' for perpetual holders (content unread) source
The research worker's summary, verbatim

BricsCAD V26 is an AutoCAD-compatible DWG CAD sold in five inheriting editions (Lite 2D; Pro 2D/3D incl. civil and point clouds; Mechanical and BIM on top of Pro; Ultimate = all). Perpetual licences are still sold from the official store (Ultimate EUR 2,640 one-time incl. first-year maintenance, renewal EUR 440/yr, optional from year two); the US perpetual price could not be captured. It runs on Windows, Linux (x86-64) and macOS (Rosetta 2, no native Apple-silicon build yet). V26 adds 2D dynamic-block authoring, 3D solid grips, property-based selection, point-cloud linear feature detection into civil strings/surfaces, IFC 4x3, Teamcenter/Vault/ProjectWise/ACC integrations and a chat assistant (BricsCAD Assist). Civil tools (Pro+) cover TIN surfaces, grading/cut-fill balance, horizontal and vertical alignments and corridors but not parcels, pipe networks, plan production or stormwater analysis. Research caveat: only six web searches succeeded and no page could be fetched directly, so rows marked unverified need confirmation.

Unique strengths

  • Perpetual licence still sold from the official store (Ultimate EUR 2,640) with maintenance optional after year one, unlike Autodesk's subscription-only model
  • One DWG platform spanning 2D, 3D direct modelling, sheet metal, BIM and civil (Ultimate) on Windows, Linux and macOS
  • AutoCAD-compatible automation surface: LISP in all editions, BRX/.NET/COM in Pro+ (unverified edition split), easing migration of LISP libraries
  • Local, non-cloud machine learning (predictive Quad) and deterministic productivity tools (Blockify, Auto-Parametrize) that do not depend on vendor servers
  • V26 enterprise integrations (Teamcenter, Vault, ProjectWise, ACC) and point-cloud extraction into civil strings/surfaces
  • Commercial DWG CAD with a supported Linux build

Notable weaknesses

  • Civil tools are preliminary/site-design level: no parcels, pipe/pressure networks, plan production, survey database, quantity takeoff or stormwater analysis found
  • macOS build is Rosetta-emulated with no native Apple-silicon build; Linux 3D acceleration unsupported on Intel GPUs and dual-GPU laptops
  • User reports of V26 crashes and slowdowns on macOS and Linux
  • US perpetual price for Ultimate not discoverable in this session; reseller USD maintenance figures disagree (USD 441 vs 484)
  • 'AI' branding contested: reviewer describes Blockify/Auto-Parametrize as deterministic pattern matching, not neural inference
  • Forum complaint about a new upgrade-and-support policy for perpetual holders (content unverified)
  • Dynamic-block authoring only arrived in V26; data-link support unknown

What they wish existed

  • A stable x.0 release each year - stop shipping regressions that force users to roll back (V25.1/25.2 crash threads).
  • A native Apple-silicon macOS build with Windows feature parity and a Metal renderer no slower than the old OpenGL path.
  • Full Wayland support and polished Linux desktop integration.
  • Better large-drawing, Structure-Browser and 3D-viewport performance.
  • Dynamic-block creation available to all users including the US, and out of 'experimental'.
  • The remaining missing or less-polished AutoCAD commands closed.
  • Keep affordable perpetual upgrades for old licences; stop raising maintenance; no push to subscription.
  • Faster, more reliable support tickets and licence/registration handling.
  • More tutorials, documentation and a larger community.
  • A plug-in API (.NET/BRX/LRX) that does not break at each yearly version.
  • Less dated UI and better rendering.
  • (Inferred) native civil tools complete enough for site design without a third-party add-on.
The review worker's summary, verbatim

User sentiment 2024-2026 is positive on the volume sites (G2 4.5/5 on 135 reviews; SaaSWorthy 4.7/5 on 131) and the pitch that works is 'AutoCAD-compatible, much cheaper, perpetual licence still sold'. The loudest negatives are stability regressions in each yearly release (V25.1-25.2 panning/autosave crashes with an MTFLAGS=0 workaround, users rolling back to meet deadlines; V26.1.08 more crashes on macOS), a macOS build users call a 'perpetual beta' (freezing, no clear native Apple-silicon binary, Metal renderer slower than OpenGL), a second-class Linux build (Wayland black window), large-drawing/3D performance, dynamic blocks (creation only arrived in V26, experimental and unavailable to US users for licensing reasons), uneven support responsiveness (Capterra support 3.9 vs ease-of-use 4.6), and a January-2026 upgrade/support policy that forum users call a 'cynical move' against old perpetual licences. No direct user reviews of the native civil tools surfaced; the market treats a third-party add-on (Civil Site Design) as the Civil 3D alternative on BricsCAD, implying the native TIN/alignment/corridor stack is incomplete for site design. LIMITS: ten searches only (shared budget exhausted) and every target host was denied by the environment's network policy, so all items are paraphrased from search-engine summaries, not opened pages; no Reddit or Hacker News threads were captured; BIM usability and LISP-compatibility themes were not reached.

Score by column

ProductFullPartialNoneUnknown
CADCraft716260

BricsCAD Ultimate2116120
Genesis (target)391000
Reading the counts. Civil 3D has the most full marks (30 of 49); its gaps are platform, price, rule checking and the sustainability rows. BricsCAD sits between (21 full) with a perpetual licence and a civil layer that stops at surfaces, alignments and grading. CADCraft (7 full) is a foundation, not a competitor. Genesis targets full marks on the rows that cost site designers the most time and money and deliberately leaves highway-scale corridor design and full 3D modelling at partial.

New versus missing, product by product

Only CADCraft has

  • An MCP server and JSON control channel: an AI agent types at the command line, calls any command as JSON, inspects the drawing and renders it, headless or inside the running app.
  • A browser build of the same engine (currently without DWG, autosave or plotting), and native builds for FreeBSD, Linux on aarch64 and riscv64, and Windows on ARM. BricsCAD ships Linux on x86-64 only; Civil 3D is Windows-only.
  • A published never-crash engineering policy enforced by compiler lints, panic guards and hostile-input regression tests.
  • Open source under MIT/Apache-2.0: auditable file handling, forkable, free to bundle with a product.

What CADCraft lacks for site work

  • Every civil object: points, surfaces, contours, alignments, profiles, corridors, grading, parcels, pipe networks, coordinate systems.
  • Lossless DWG exchange and a real-file corpus; sheet sets; eTransmit; XCLIP; dynamic-block authoring; 3D.
  • GIS, LandXML, DEM, raster and PDF underlays; printers beyond PDF.
  • A production user base: 6.3k stars and about 390 open upstream issues show people trying real office files (#1041, #738, #1636), but there are no switched-from-AutoCAD reports and no known project run on it yet.

Only Civil 3D has

  • A survey database with least-squares adjustment, parcels with map check, criteria-based alignments and superelevation, corridors with subassemblies, gravity and pressure pipe networks with parts catalogues, integrated drainage analysis, sections and quantity takeoff, plan production, Dynamo and a .NET API, country kits.
  • Civil data references shared across drawings (data shortcuts, Vault).

Only BricsCAD has

  • A perpetual licence with optional maintenance, a supported Linux build, and BIM, sheet metal and mechanical design in the same DWG application.
  • Blockify, Auto-Parametrize and a locally running predictive Quad, plus BRX compatibility for ObjectARX plug-ins.

Only Genesis targets

  • Zoning and site-rule checks, solar and rainwater sizing, greywater, septic, wells, cisterns, earth and timber building types, and public data feeds cached in a self-contained project.
  • A published crash count and a reference engine whose links carry health, hashes and GUIDs.

6What users praise and curse, pooled

The same complaints recur across the three products, the open-source alternatives and the industry-wide stability notes. This section pools them into twenty canonical themes so that section 4.5 can answer each one with a design. A cell shows the worst severity a worker assigned to that theme for that product, with the number of distinct complaint rows behind it; an empty cell means no reviewer raised it, not that the product is free of it. Severity is the reviewers' weight, not ours: high costs people data, money or days; medium costs hours; low is irritation.

What would make reviewers switch to an open-source CAD

  • Open the office's real DWGs (2004-2018, xrefs, layouts, dynamic blocks, associative constraints) exactly, and save files that AutoCAD and BricsCAD open with zero AUDIT errors and no version downgrade (cadcraft #604, #1041, #907; LibreCAD #3064).
  • Never silently drop entities, xdata or draw order on save; warn before any lossy write and keep the original untouched (CADCraft README caveat; #540).
  • Stay smooth on 20-50 MB drawings and multi-xref plans: background loading, visibility culling, first paint under ~12 s, peak memory under ~2.5 GB (#1076, #1625, #738; 'AutoCAD handles files 4x larger').
  • Zero crashes plus real recovery: atomic fsync-durable saves, .bak files, recovery journal, single-instance-per-drawing locks, conflict detection, and AUDIT/RECOVER equivalents (#540, #752; LibreCAD 2.2.1.6 crash list; FreeCAD 362 crash issues).
  • Robust startup on any GPU with a software/fallback renderer when Metal/Vulkan/DX12/OpenGL fails (#1724, #1631, #955, README AMD note).
  • AutoCAD-compatible muscle memory: command aliases, right-click Enter, clicks registered while the mouse moves, familiar selection, DIMSTYLE and PLOT dialogs, dockable panels and saved workspaces (nanoCAD ease score; #1001, #729, #850, #429; LibreCAD #3162).
  • Proper external references: relative paths, re-path without crashing, clipping, in-place edit, overrides preserved, raster images listed, fast load (#1636, #1581, #813, #886, #1756, #1625).
  • Reliable plotting in any unit and paper size with a working preview (#1828, #1791; LibreCAD printing reviews).
  • Integrated, current help and a support channel where the reporter actually sees the answer (LibreCAD docs reviews, Capterra support 3.1/5; CADCraft Discord relay).
  • Native site-work objects instead of a lagging add-on: TIN surfaces, contours, alignments/profiles, CSV/LandXML point import, coordinate systems and GIS layers (FreeCAD Trails status; QGIS-as-complement).
  • A public real-file test corpus with per-release round-trip reports and AUDIT counts (cadcraft #1041 offer; LibreCAD PR #2855 method).
  • Linux/macOS parity with signed installers, no compile-it-yourself (LibreCAD unsigned DMG note; BRL-CAD reviews).
  • A funding model users already accept: one-time ~$50 Pro with a year of updates (QCAD) rather than subscription.
The open-source review worker's summary, verbatim

Across review sites and issue trackers (2024-2026) the free CAD field is judged on one question: do the office's real DWGs open exactly and come back clean. Every product fails it somewhere: LibreCAD maintainers say DWG support is 'still being implemented' (2.2.2-alpha); QCAD puts DWG behind the ~$50 Pro edition; FreeCAD's DXF/DWG path has silent export failures and it is widely called 'not an AutoCAD replacement' for 2D; CADCraft opens more DWGs than LibreCAD per a LibreCAD user, but BricsCAD V26 refused 14 of 23 CADCraft-saved DWGs and AutoCAD AUDIT finds errors after a round trip. Stability is the second theme: FreeCAD has 362 crash-titled GitHub issues since 2025-01-01 and topological naming is mitigated, not solved; LibreCAD logged 37 (mostly fixed fast; 2.2.1.6 is a crash-fix release); CADCraft has GPU-specific startup failures, an xref re-path crash and a reproduced silent data loss when two instances save one DWG. Performance on 5-50 MB drawings and xrefs is third (CADCraft: 47.8 MB DWG 20-28 s and ~4.5 GB; a 77 MB xref peaks at 8.4 GB). Praise is consistent: free, lightweight, fast maintainer response, QCAD's simplicity and one-time price, nanoCAD's AutoCAD-like ease (G2 8.9 vs 8.3). No open-source CAD offers civil objects except FreeCAD's one-developer Trails add-on; site designers use QGIS as a complement and Blender-GIS (~9.5k stars) for terrain. Caveat: Capterra/G2/AlternativeTo/QCAD-forum numbers are from search snippets (pages unreachable); SourceForge and GitHub figures were read live on 2026-10-21; Reddit/HN could not be opened.

The industry worker read vendor knowledge bases, release notes and forum threads about crashes, references, locking, packaging and recovery across AutoCAD-family products. Its market and price findings are in section 4.6.

Perpetual licensing still exists at the low endBricsCAD, Carlson, 12d and Trimble still sell perpetual seats; surveyors cite '3 seats of Carlson IntelliCAD for the price of a single seat of C3d' and switching to BricsCAD after 20+ years of AutoCAD over the pricing model.rpls.com/forums/software-cad-mapping/surveyin… (via sibling notes); thatcadgirl.com/faq/what-to-know-about-purcha…

Autodesk crash reporting is systematicCER sends a minidump, recent command history and system info; reports are triaged by severity and number of customers and reporters are notified when fixed. A new app should match this from day one.source

Relative paths are now the defaultAutoCAD's default xref path type is relative (REFPATHTYPE) and eTransmit's 'organized folder structure' converts absolute paths inside the root to relative, so a project folder can be moved as a unit when everything lives inside it.help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2026/PTB/?guid=GUI…; help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/PTB/?guid=GUI…
CADCraft's engine-level crash discipline is a usable baseNo panics in production code, catch_unwind around every command and interactive step with the drawing restored, 100 hostile-named test functions (39 in the engine crate; recounted 2026-10-21), input caps, background autosave with instance lock files and GPU-fault autosave, AUDIT that never guesses at content (sibling inventory).cadcraft-referencing.md (sibling notes); /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/guard.rs

What still goes wrong

ThemeDetailSev.Source
Crash volume is structural, not incidentalAutodesk's own fixed-issue lists carry ~17 crash/hang fixes in Civil 3D 2027.1 alone, ~12 in 2027, ~15 in 2026.1 and ~11 in 2026.2; 2024.3 fixed two cases where alignment data was lost after save and reopen. Forum users report weekly to daily crashes in 2026.highhelp.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=c…; help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2026/ENU/?guid=c…; forums.autodesk.com/t5/civil-3d-forum/civil-3…
Crashes on save and open lose workSeparate KB articles cover fatal errors on save (corrupt AEC objects; remedy is to export everything to LandXML and re-import, or fall back to the .sv$), on save when proxy graphics exhaust memory, on open from corrupt xrefs or back-saved files; a crash mid-save can leave a truncated DWG with no footer.highautodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; forums.augi.com/showthread.php?176841-Unable-…
Data shortcuts are per-user path files with no lockingEach shortcut XML stores a path; in ACC/Forma projects member permissions rewrite that path per user ('truncating or expanding the path ... making it unavailable for others'); 2027.1 fixed host drawings mutating shortcut XMLs 'may not cover all cases'; Autodesk docs state there is no locking for concurrent shortcut edits and folder-structure changes may be lost; syncing over WAN can take hours and 'Repair' hangs.highautodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2023/ENU/?guid=G…; help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=c…
Xref paths break on move, drive mapping, permissions, cloud cache or VPNRelative paths are unavailable across drives or servers and can fail because 'automatic save is too frequent'; unresolved-xref causes include mapped-vs-UNC, drive letters hijacked by USB devices, paths over 256 chars, stopped DNS services; after a server migration opening drawings can freeze, with the advice to unplug the network cable; the Reference Manager bulk-repaths with no undo.highhelp.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/ENU/?guid=GUI…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…
Cloud worksharing discards local workDesktop Connector sync failures leave files 'Modified' and invisible to co-workers; a 'Conflict' gives priority to the cloud copy and local changes go to the Recycle Bin; 'Free Up Space' before sync deletes unsynced work; file locking is unsupported on long paths; the shortcut-sync balloon can appear before upload finishes so clicking it loads stale data.highautodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2023/ENU/?guid=C…
DWG bloat and corruption are routineAutodesk lists cloud-sync folders, non-Microsoft networks, NAS/SAN, VPN drops, email and third-party DWG writers as corruption sources; cleanup is a ritual (detach xrefs, OVERKILL, -PURGE regapps, reset scale list, WBLOCK into a new file); files stay ~8 MB after deleting everything; RECOVER can itself stall at 99% or be demanded on every open; field reports of 20 MB to 1.6 GB after pasting a corridor and >100,000 RegApps.mediumautodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; forums.autodesk.com/t5/civil-3d-forum/urgent-… (via sibling notes)
Autosave and backup have fine print that defeats themThe autosave timer resets on every manual save so frequent savers may never get an .sv$; autosave stays off if a LISP routine or the Block Editor crashes mid-command; only one .bak generation is kept and none is written when saving to Forma Data Management since 2022.1; the Recovery Manager ignores never-saved drawings; full-time CRC validation is off by default because it slows opening.mediumhelp.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/ENU/?guid=GUI…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…
Packaging and binding are lossyeTransmit omits SHX/TTF fonts, .pat, .lin, .pmp, OLE links and URL references by default and can silently drop files; Bind renames every named object to name$0$name while Insert silently lets the local definition override the bound one; bind defects (parcel labels to '???', missing profile band labels) persisted across releases.mediumhelp.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/PTB/?guid=GUI…; help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/PLK/?guid=GUI…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…
File locking is delegated to the OS and goes staleSince AutoCAD 2000 the program does not control locking; .dwl/.dwl2 exist only for WHOHAS, survive crashes and cause 'in use or read-only' until deleted by hand; cloud drives 'often do not have any type of file locking'.mediumautodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…
Fault isolation is done by the userStandard remedies for fatal errors are: install updates, reset the user profile, update the GPU driver, uninstall plug-ins, purge/audit/recover, WBLOCK, clean reinstall; many crash classes are add-in, object-enabler or .NET/VC++ runtime interactions rather than core geometry.mediumautodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; forums.autodesk.com/t5/autocad-forum/crash-in… (via sibling notes)
Subscription-only pricing and billing angerCivil 3D at USD 2,870/yr list with no perpetual option; Vectorworks and SketchUp also subscription-only; Autodesk the company scores 1.5/5 on Trustpilot over auto-renewal billing; Flex tokens burn on idle sessions; network licences retired.mediumautodesk.com/solutions/aec/civil-3d-subscript…; uk.trustpilot.com/review/autodesk.com (via sibling notes); openlm.com/blog/autodesk-flex-is-it-the-right… (via sibling notes)

Evidence gaps in this runBricsCAD, Bentley, Vectorworks and Carlson crash threads, G2/Capterra review text, BLS buyer counts and most open-source funding pages could not be opened (search budget exhausted after 12 queries; fetches proxy-blocked); those items are flagged unverified in the notes.lowmarket-and-stability.md section 0

4Genesis: the new open-source land-development CAD

Genesis (working name) is the land-development and site-design CAD we intend to build on CADCraft's engine: a tool for small civil and survey firms, landscape architects, design-build contractors, planners, and the growing number of owner-builders, homesteaders and permaculture designers who today either rent Civil 3D for a few projects a year or do site work in spreadsheets and sketches. It covers a site from due diligence to as-built, treats old-school techniques (metes and bounds, cisterns, passive solar, earth walls) and new ones (solar arrays, rainwater harvesting, bioretention, microgrids) as first-class objects, and is scoped so that it can be finished: lots, pads, driveways, small streets, grading, drainage and utilities to the main, not highways, rail or city networks.

It stays separate from CADCraft (#1), which continues toward AutoCAD parity, and consumes CADCraft's crates as libraries. It competes with Civil 3D (#2) on the work small sites actually need, and with BricsCAD (#3) on price and platform, while being the only one of the four that models sustainability and self-sufficiency on a site.

Identity card (targets, not measurements)

Name
Genesis (working name; the final name and brand are the owner's decision)
Licence and price
MIT OR Apache-2.0 like CADCraft; the desktop app free with no gated features; paid hosting, support and packs (4.6)
Platforms
Windows, macOS, Linux and the browser from one Rust codebase (inherited); same installers as CADCraft
Scope
Sites: lots, pads, driveways, small streets, grading, drainage, utilities to the main, planting, solar and water self-sufficiency; not highways, rail or city networks
Native format
A project folder or .genesis zip with a JSON manifest; DWG/DXF/LandXML/GeoPackage as exchange
Commands
About 150 inherited drafting commands plus about 100 civil commands, a core of 60 covering 90% of site work; everything a command, JSON form never opens a dialog
Assistant
Bring your own key, any provider, outside the app first (MCP), inside the app from phase 5
Status
A plan with a roadmap (4.7); nothing has been built

The five goals, made concrete

GoalWhat it means in the productHow we will know
SimplerA core of sixty commands covers ninety percent of site work (about a hundred civil commands in all); one project file; opinionated defaults; styles are templates, not programming.A trained drafter takes a one-acre commercial site from survey to a permit-ready plan set in under two working days; a new user completes the tutorial site in a day without training videos.
Better referencing and data connectionsEvery link is a typed, versioned reference in a manifest: drawings, surfaces, point files, GIS layers, spreadsheets, PDFs, and live public data feeds (terrain, soils, flood, rainfall, solar).Open any project on any machine and every reference resolves or reports exactly why not.
Containment of files, copying and linksThe project is the package: one folder or one zip holds the drawings, their references, cached data and a journal. Copying a project, or objects between projects, carries everything they depend on and reports what was merged.Zip a project, mail it, unzip it elsewhere: zero broken links, zero missing styles.
Faster referencingReferences load on worker threads from a parsed cache keyed by content; reloads are pointer swaps; each reference has its own spatial index; big bases load on demand by viewport.Re-opening a project with a 50 MB survey base is sub-second warm, and a changed reference is noticed without a Reload button.
Zero crashesInherit CADCraft's never-crash wall (no panics, guards that restore the drawing, caps on hostile input) and add what it lacks: a CI gate on every change, a command journal for keystroke-level recovery, reader fuzzing, web autosave and opt-in crash telemetry.Crash means any unhandled panic or hang over five seconds; the target is zero in the field and the number is published.
Why not just add civil features to CADCraft? Because the two targets pull in different directions: CADCraft measures itself against 491 AutoCAD menu items, Genesis against a permit-ready plan set. Sharing crates keeps the engineering common; separating the products keeps each simple.

4.1Principles mapped to the goals

Each principle traces to one of the five goals and ends with the measure that tells us whether it is kept.

GoalPrincipleDesignMeasure
SimplerScope to sites, not highwaysLots, driveways, pads, swales, ponds, small streets, utilities to the main; no superelevation tables, no rail, no corridor subassembly programming.About 100 civil commands; a core of 60 covers 90% of site work
SimplerOne container, one mental modelA project is a folder (or zip) with project.json, drawings, references and cache. No data shortcuts, no Vault, no sheet set database on the side.No separate 'reference manager' app
SimplerDefaults that pass reviewShip label styles, layer standards (NCS-compatible), sheet templates and unit conventions for US survey feet and metric so the first plan set looks professional.Default plan set accepted by a design-partner jurisdiction
SimplerProgressive disclosureEvery civil object has three levels: draw it, adjust it with grips and a properties card, open the full editor. Dialogs are never required; the JSON form of every command never opens one (inherited rule).Each object has all three levels
SimplerAn assistant that can drive the appInherit the MCP server so a local or hosted AI agent can set up a project, import data, and explain a failed check; the agent uses the same commands as the user.MCP tool coverage of 100% of commands
ReferencingTyped references with healthReference kinds: drawing (xref), surface, point file, GIS layer, table (CSV/XLSX), PDF/raster underlay, data feed. Each records project-relative path, content hash, size, mtime, role, and last status: loaded, unloaded, not found, refused (why), changed since load.linkcheck reports every reference in one table
ReferencingStable identitiesEvery object carries a GUID that survives save, copy and DWG round trip; references point at GUIDs, not handles, so renumbering by another program breaks nothing.Round-trip corpus keeps 100% of GUIDs
ReferencingData feeds are references tooUSGS elevation, NRCS soils, FEMA flood zones, NOAA Atlas 14 rainfall, NREL solar resource and county parcels attach like xrefs: fetched once, cached in the project, re-fetched on demand, with provenance and licence recorded.Feed snapshot stored with hash and date
ReferencingTwo-way data linksTables and schedules link to CSV/XLSX with write-back; quantities, point lists and pipe schedules export and re-import without losing identity.Edit in spreadsheet, see it in the drawing
ContainmentThe project is the package'Package' (eTransmit) becomes copying the folder: all paths are project-relative and the manifest lists everything. A single-file .genesis zip is the same folder compressed.Package = zip, no path rewriting needed
ContainmentOne merge functionPaste, insert, import, bind and copy-project share one merge_definitions with CADCraft's deterministic rule (target's named definitions win; anonymous ones renamed; dependencies carried) and return a merge report.Merge report in every result
ContainmentKeep the previous versionAtomic save plus a rolling set of previous versions and an append-only journal inside the container; Drawing Recovery replays the journal to the last command.Recovery to the last command, not the last ten minutes
SpeedParsed-reference cacheReferences are parsed once per content hash and shared by pointer (CADCraft's copy-on-write store makes this free); a file watcher marks them stale.Warm re-reference < 1 s for 50 MB
SpeedWork off the UI threadOpen, reference load, surface build, hydrology runs and plots all run on workers with progress and cancel; the canvas never blocks.No UI stall > 100 ms in the benchmark suite
SpeedNative format first, DWG as exchangeAn open, documented native format (JSON manifest plus a compact binary entity store) avoids the 6× DXF inflation of the DWG bridge; DWG/DXF/LandXML/GeoPackage are import/export, with unknown data kept opaque and written back.10 MB DWG opens in < 10 s
Zero crashesInherit the wallunsafe forbidden; unwrap/expect/panic! denied by lint; Result everywhere; catch_unwind at every boundary with restore-from-snapshot; non-finite results refused; caps on every input-sized loop; hostile-input tests per feature.Lint wall on every crate from commit one
Zero crashesGate every changeCI runs fmt, clippy with warnings denied, the whole test suite, the real-file corpus and the wasm build on every pull request (CADCraft's biggest stability gap is that it does not).No merge without green CI
ContainmentOne writer per drawingAn instance lock names who has the file open; a second instance opens read-only with a 'who has it' notice; an external modification since load is detected before save; a save never silently overwrites another program's write (upstream #540 is the counter-example).Two instances cannot both save the same file silently
SpeedMemory budgetStreaming readers for DWG, DXF, LandXML and DEMs instead of an in-memory text bridge; peak memory on open is bounded to a small multiple of the file size and shown in the status bar.A 50 MB drawing opens under 2.5 GB peak
Zero crashesStart on any GPUIf GPU initialisation fails, the app starts on the CPU rasteriser (CADCraft already has one for export) and says so, instead of a black window; the GPU path can be re-enabled per machine.Starts on a machine with no working GPU driver
Zero crashesFault isolationThe GPU renderer and every plug-in run out of process (or as WASM) so a driver or add-on fault cannot take the document down; crash reports include the drawing excerpt and the tail of the command journal.A killed renderer process never loses an edit
Zero crashesMeasure itOpt-in crash and hang reports, a public counter, and a 'days since last crash' badge; every field crash lands with a regression test.Public crash counter

4.2Land-development feature set

The workflow of a site, stage by stage, with the deliverables each stage owes and the Genesis commands that produce them. Everything is a command, as in CADCraft: typed at the prompt, clicked in the task bar, scripted, or called by an agent over MCP with JSON and never a dialog.

StageDeliverablesGenesis commands
Feasibility and due diligenceParcel from county GIS, zoning overlay, flood zone, soils, wetlands, slope map, utility availability, a yield study.projectnew, parcelfetch, feed attach (USGS, FEMA, NRCS, NWI), slopemap, yield
Boundary and topographic surveyImport points and field books, draw the boundary by metes and bounds, build the existing-ground surface, contours, breaklines, map check.pointsimport, cogo, legal (write/read a legal description), surfacecreate, contours, mapcheck
Site planning and zoning checksBuilding pads, setbacks, easements, parking, ADA routes, impervious cover, tree protection, sight distance, fire access.setback, pad, parking, adaroute, rulecheck (jurisdiction rule sheet), impervious
Grading and earthworkProposed surface from feature lines, slopes to daylight, retaining walls, cut/fill balance, haul.featureline, gradeto, pad, wall, volumes, balance
Drainage and stormwaterCatchments, time of concentration, rational and NRCS methods, pipes and inlets, detention and bioretention sizing, LID credits, erosion-control plan.catchment, rain (NOAA Atlas 14), runoff, detention, bioretention, swppp
UtilitiesWater, sanitary, storm, dry utilities with profiles, crossings and clearances; septic and wells where there is no main.pipe, pressurepipe, profileview, crossings, septic, well
Roads, driveways and parkingHorizontal and vertical alignment, simple cross-section templates, curb returns, sight triangles, turning paths.alignment, profile, section, corridor (templates only), turning
Sustainability and self-sufficiencySolar array layout and yield, rainwater harvesting, greywater, passive-solar orientation, wind and shade studies, wildfire and flood resilience.solar, sunpath, shade, rainharvest, greywater, defensiblespace
Plan production and permittingSheet index, plan and profile sheets, details, revision clouds, plat, quantities, submittal package.sheets, plat, quantities, revcloud (inherited), submittal
Construction staking and as-builtsStakeout point export, cut sheets, as-built import and comparison, final plat.stakeout, cutsheet, asbuilt, compare (inherited)

The command set (111 civil commands on top of roughly 150 inherited drafting commands; a core of 60 covers 90% of site work)

4.3Old-school and neo techniques as first-class objects

Two tables: the techniques people built with for centuries and still permit today, and the ones arriving now. For each, the column that matters is what the CAD needs: an object type, a calculation, a data feed or a report. Genesis would model both sets natively; none of the three shipping products models either beyond generic geometry.

Old-school techniques

TechniqueWhat the CAD needsSource
COGO by bearing and distanceBearing/azimuth/DMS parser and formatter; create point/line by bearing-distance; inverse; bearing-bearing and distance-distance intersections; traverse with closure and compass-rule adjustment; mapcheck reportsource
