How a feed works. feed attach usgs-3dep fetches the elevation tiles for the project's bounding box, stores them under cache/ with the request, date, hash and licence in the manifest, and builds the existing-ground surface from them. The project works offline afterwards; feed refresh re-fetches and reports what changed. Rainfall (NOAA Atlas 14), soils (NRCS), flood zones (FEMA NFHL), solar resource (NREL NSRDB/PVWatts), wetlands (USFWS NWI) and parcels (county open data) attach the same way. Paid or licence-restricted sources are plug-ins, never bundled.

4.4Architecture and what Genesis inherits from CADCraft

Genesis would keep CADCraft's layering rule (lower layers never see the UI) and its quality gates, inherit the crates below, and add seven new ones. The crate names are proposals. The dependency model is library first: the crates marked keep are consumed as pinned dependencies from a tagged CADCraft release and upgraded deliberately, so CADCraft fixes flow in without a merge; only engine and ui-egui are forked, because Genesis prunes and reshapes them.

CrateLayerDecisionWhat changes
geomL0keep and extend2D/3D vectors, arcs, splines, intersections, tessellation. Add true 3D points, a tolerance context relative to drawing extents, and known-answer tests at survey coordinates (1e6–1e7).
dxf, dwgL0/L3keep as exchangeDXF reader/writer and the acadrust DWG bridge stay import/export paths; the native format is Genesis's own. Unknown data stays opaque and is written back.
color, docL1keepColour model and the copy-on-write entity store: the store's O(1) snapshots are what make undo, autosave, panic-restore and reference sharing free. Add GUIDs and a reference table.
fonts, renderL2keepStroke font, TrueType layout, display lists, CPU raster. Add surface and contour rendering, hillshade, and label collision for plan sheets.
constraintsL2keepParametric solver reused for parcel and pad constraints.
ioL3keep and extendPDF/SVG/PNG export, plot styles, templates. Add LandXML, GeoJSON/GeoPackage/Shapefile, DEM/GeoTIFF, CSV/XLSX two-way.
engineL4keep the kernel, prune the AutoCAD surfaceSession, command registry, prompts, snaps, selection, undo, spatial index, autosave, audit, guards: keep. Keep only the commands site work needs (about 150 of 431) and add the civil layer.
mcpL5keepThe MCP server and control channel are a differentiator; extend tools with project, reference and civil queries.
ui-eguiL5replace the chrome, keep the canvasThe egui canvas, GPU path and palettes stay; the AutoCAD-shaped menu tree, Tool Sets and Ribbon-like density are replaced by a task bar organised by the workflow stages above.
new: projectL3buildContainer, manifest, references, feeds, link health, journal, package, versions.
new: geoL1buildCoordinate reference systems (EPSG), projections and datum transforms, geolocation, basemaps.
new: terrainL2buildTIN (Delaunay with breaklines), grids, contours, volumes, slope/aspect, watersheds, DEM import.
new: civilL4buildPoints, COGO, parcels, legal descriptions, alignments, profiles, sections, feature lines, grading, pipes.
new: hydroL2buildRational and NRCS runoff, time of concentration, inlet and culvert hydraulics, detention routing, LID sizing, rainwater harvesting.
new: solarL2buildSun paths, shading, array layout, PVWatts/NSRDB client with cached results.
new: rulesL4buildJurisdiction rule sheets (setbacks, cover, parking ratios) as data; rulecheck evaluates them against the model.

The project container

A project is a folder (or a zip of that folder with the extension .genesis) containing project.json, the drawings, a refs/ folder for referenced files that live with the project, cache/ for feed snapshots and parsed references, versions/ for previous saves and journal/ for the append-only command log. Paths in the manifest are relative to the project root; a reference outside the project is allowed but flagged, and projectpackage copies it in. The stored-path policy inherited from CADCraft (craft-docpath: only this machine or the project's own share, refusals are statuses) applies to every path.

{
  "genesis": "1.0",
  "project": { "name": "Oak Ridge Lot 12", "crs": "EPSG:2277", "units": "us-survey-foot",
               "created": "2026-10-21T18:40:00Z", "guid": "7b6a…" },
  "drawings": [ { "path": "sheets/site-plan.gen", "role": "site plan", "guid": "a1…" } ],
  "references": [
    { "kind": "drawing", "path": "refs/boundary-survey.dwg", "role": "survey base",
      "sha256": "9f3…", "size": 48213344, "mtime": "2026-09-30T14:02:11Z",
      "status": "loaded", "loaded_hash": "9f3…" },
    { "kind": "surface", "path": "refs/eg.landxml", "role": "existing ground", "sha256": "c0…" },
    { "kind": "feed", "provider": "noaa-atlas14", "query": { "lat": 30.27, "lon": -97.74 },
      "cache": "cache/atlas14.json", "fetched": "2026-10-21", "licence": "public domain" },
    { "kind": "feed", "provider": "usgs-3dep", "query": { "bbox": [...], "res": "1m" },
      "cache": "cache/3dep.tif", "fetched": "2026-10-21", "licence": "public domain" },
    { "kind": "table", "path": "data/pipe-schedule.xlsx", "range": "Schedule!A1:H40", "two_way": true }
  ],
  "resources": [
    { "kind": "font", "name": "craft-sans", "path": "resources/craft-sans.ttf", "licence": "OFL-1.1" },
    { "kind": "linetype", "name": "site-standard", "path": "resources/site.lin" },
    { "kind": "plotstyle", "name": "permit-mono", "path": "resources/permit-mono.json" }
  ],
  "lock": { "holder": "brandon@laptop", "pid": 4812, "since": "2026-10-21T18:41:07Z" },
  "journal": "journal/site-plan.log",
  "versions": "versions/"
}

Reference engine

  • Load: on a worker thread, through a process-wide parsed cache keyed by (canonical path, size, mtime, sha256). A hit is a pointer copy thanks to the copy-on-write store; a miss parses once. Nested references resolve relative to their own file, bounded by depth 16, a cycle check and byte/object caps, exactly as CADCraft does today.
  • Status: every reference carries loaded / unloaded / not found / refused (reason) / changed since load / unreferenced, persisted in the manifest (CADCraft loses 'unloaded' on reopen; Genesis would keep it).
  • Watch: a file watcher marks references stale and offers reload; linkcheck prints one table for the whole project and is the first thing projectopen runs.
  • Index: each loaded reference keeps its own R-tree so picking and snapping through references never expands their blocks.
  • Demand loading: references larger than a threshold load by viewport extents (tiles for DEMs and point clouds, spatial chunks for drawings).
  • Identity: objects have GUIDs; a reference to a surface, alignment or point group points at a GUID; handles are an implementation detail of exchange formats.

Concurrency and durability

  • One writer: opening a project takes a lock recorded in the manifest (holder, process, time); a second instance opens read-only and shows who has it; a stale lock from a crash is detected by process liveness and offered for takeover.
  • External change: before every save the file's hash is compared with the hash at load; a mismatch stops the save and offers compare, overwrite or save-as, never a silent overwrite.
  • Atomic save: write to a temporary file, fsync, rename; the previous version moves to versions/ (rolling count and size budget); the journal is truncated only after the rename succeeds.
  • Recovery: on start, an unfinished journal replays onto the last version to the last completed command and asks before keeping the result.
  • Resources travel: fonts, linetypes, hatch patterns and plot styles the drawings use are listed under resources and copied by projectpackage, so a package never prints differently elsewhere.

Data model notes

  • 3D f64 points everywhere (CADCraft's kernel is 2D), with a tolerance context scaled to drawing extents so that state-plane coordinates in the millions behave.
  • Civil objects are dynamic: a surface rebuilds when its points or breaklines change; a profile follows its alignment and surface; a pipe network's profile view follows the pipes; labels follow objects. Dependencies are explicit edges so rebuilds are incremental and off-thread.
  • Unknown data is sacred: anything imported that Genesis has no model for is kept opaque and written back, in the native format and in DXF/DWG.
  • Rule sheets (zoning, stormwater, fire access) are data files per jurisdiction, versioned in the project, evaluated by rulecheck; the first ones are written with design partners.

The assistant: bring your own key, any model

The assistant arrives in two phases. In phase 0 to 4 it is outside the app: any MCP client with the user's own key drives Genesis exactly as it drives CADCraft today, and every run is recorded. From phase 5 it is inside the app, with the same commands and the same records, once the core has met its crash and corpus gates; no in-app model work displaces stability work.

The assistant mayThe assistant must never
Run any command the user could run, with the JSON form (no dialogs), and show the command it ranRun a command without recording it in the journal, or save, package or delete without a confirmation step the user configured
Read the project manifest, link health, rule-check results and the drawing excerpt it needsSend drawing content, project paths or keys to a provider the user has not enabled for that purpose
Explain a failed check, propose a fix, and apply it when askedChange a reference's path, a rule sheet or a coordinate system on its own initiative
Compare models on the same task and keep the recordHide which model answered, or train on a user's project without an explicit opt-in

CADCraft is already driven by outside agents over MCP with whatever account the client has; Genesis would make that explicit inside the app. The user plugs in their own provider key (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Mistral, xAI, DeepSeek, Groq, OpenRouter, Together, Cohere, Azure, Bedrock, Vertex, or a local Ollama / LM Studio / llama.cpp server), chooses a model per role (command help, drawing review, long agent runs, offline), and the assistant uses the same commands as the user. Keys stay in the OS keychain, nothing leaves the machine without a policy, and every run is recorded per model so the models can be compared on the same tasks. The provider table with links to get keys, the proposed providers.toml, the privacy rules and the iteration-record schema are in docs/genesis/ai/byok.md and docs/genesis/ai/training/iterations/, together with the knowledge pack the assistant is trained and evaluated on.

Migrating a project

  • From AutoCAD or BricsCAD: projectnew --from folder/ reads the drawings, resolves their xrefs, images, fonts and plot styles with CADCraft's resolver, copies what it finds into refs/ and resources/, and writes a manifest whose first linkcheck lists what it could not find.
  • From Civil 3D: surfaces, alignments, profiles, parcels and pipe networks arrive through LandXML, which Civil 3D exports natively; the drawing's geometry comes through DWG; AECC objects that have no LandXML form stay as proxy geometry and are listed.
  • From a bare DWG folder: every drawing becomes a project drawing; every path found becomes a reference with a status; nothing is rewritten inside the DWGs.
  • Back out: dwgexport writes AutoCAD 2018 DWG with the unknown data it kept, plus LandXML for the civil objects, so a project is never trapped.

IndustryCloud worksharing discards local workhighProject zip sharing first; later a local-first hosted sync that never discards a local version (keeps both on conflict)○autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…; help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2023/ENU/?guid=C…
IndustryOpen-source commercialisation is fragilelowBusiness model: open core plus services (section 4.6); no VC dependence for the desktop tool○80.lv/articles/blender-reports-operating-at-a…; ondsel.com/blog/goodbye/ (unverified)
Open-source CADFreeCAD topological naming mitigated, not solvedhighNot applicable: Genesis has no parametric 3D kernel; site objects depend on explicit edges, not face names○source

Improvements the goals require beyond the pooled complaint themes

  • A command journal so recovery replays to the last command, not to the last autosave.
  • Merge reports: every paste, insert, import and bind returns what was kept, renamed or dropped.
  • A public crash counter and a crash-free-days badge in the About window.
  • Feed provenance: every dataset the plan relies on is listed with date, licence and hash on the plan's data sheet.
  • A headless mode that produces the full plan set and quantities from the project (inherited CLI), so plan checks run in CI for firms that want it.

4.6Market, pricing and go-to-market

Civil/infrastructure design software is a ~USD 2-4 B/yr market growing ~7%/yr (analyst figures disagree by 60%). Serious civil tools cost USD 1.2k-6k per seat per year; Autodesk, Vectorworks, SketchUp and Land F/X are subscription-only, leaving BricsCAD, Carlson, 12d, Bentley (at ~2.5x annual) and Trimble dongles as the last perpetual options - the clearest pricing gap. Open-source tools live on small recurring donations plus a few corporate patrons (Blender: ~EUR 3.1 M income in FY2024 yet a loss), low-priced pro builds (QCAD, Krita store builds, Ardour pay-what-you-want) or a separate services company (Godot/W4); the VC-backed FreeCAD company Ondsel shut down in Dec 2024 (unverified). On stability, Autodesk's own release notes list 10-17 crash/hang fixes per Civil 3D point release through 2026-2027, its knowledge base has a separate FATAL ERROR article per workflow, and references are path strings in DWGs and per-user XML files that break on every move, rename, drive mapping, permission or cloud-cache difference; each mitigation (autosave, .bak, Recovery Manager, Reference Manager, eTransmit, Bind, Desktop Connector) carries documented fine print, and cloud worksharing turns 'file not found' into 'local changes discarded'. Caveat: the shared search budget ran out after 12 searches and page fetches were proxy-blocked, so BricsCAD/Bentley/Vectorworks crash evidence and several funding pages are unverified; Autodesk evidence comes from the official Autodesk help tool and sibling notes.

Positioning

  • Against Civil 3D: everything a site needs at a fraction of the complexity, no subscription, no Windows requirement, and a crash record you can check.
  • Against BricsCAD: civil objects that are real (surfaces, grading, drainage, parcels with legal descriptions), sustainability modelling, and open source.
  • Against spreadsheets and sketches: a plan set a reviewer accepts, from data the project fetched for you.

Proposed price points

For the services around the free app, set against what the same buyer pays today. These are starting points for the design-partner conversations, not decisions.

Business model options (open-source core is non-negotiable)

Revenue lineWhat is soldPrecedentNote

Opportunities the market research flagged

  • Business: free, perpetual, open desktop app; monetise a paid hosted project container + link-health service only after the desktop tool is in daily use (Ondsel lesson); paid support/training for firms and municipalities; sponsorship from survey-hardware, workstation and GPU vendors plus a small-donor programme (Blender model); later a plug-in marketplace; never dual licensing or core feature paywalls.
  • Stability: keep CADCraft's no-panic/catch_unwind/hostile-test discipline and add a CI gate on every PR; atomic saves with N-generation backups and a continuous change journal instead of a resettable 10-minute timer; autosave on web; per-object integrity hashes always on.
  • Stability: sandbox plug-ins (separate process or WASM) so add-ins, enablers and runtimes cannot take the host down; opt-in crash telemetry that files a reproducible report with the drawing excerpt.
  • Files: no RegApp/NOD-style bloat by construction, deterministic save, per-table size report; documented versioned object schema with forward and backward readers so 'save to older version' exists from day one.
  • Referencing: references by content identity plus project-relative path recorded in a project manifest (xrefs, data links, images, fonts, templates, plot styles with hash/size/mtime/role); relocation, packaging (fonts, patterns, linetypes included) and eTransmit become a manifest copy.
  • Referencing: a linkcheck command reporting found/missing/refused/changed-since-load for every nested link, a file watcher raising 'reload available', persisted reference state (Unloaded, found-at), colour-coded link health in the palette.
  • Collaboration: shared design objects referenced by ID through the manifest rather than per-user XML path files; explicit lock/merge semantics; local-first sync that never discards local work (keep both versions on conflict).
  • Containment: one movable project folder; copy = deep copy with link rewrite; paste/insert/bind merge rules (existing name wins, anonymous names regenerated) shown in a preview so nothing changes silently.

Launch plan

  • Design partners: five firms (two civil, one survey, one landscape, one design-build) and two owner-builders, each contributing a real project to the corpus under licence.
  • First proof: one permit-ready site plan accepted by a real jurisdiction, produced end to end in Genesis, published as a case study with the project container.
  • Marketing lines come from the pooled complaints (section 6) and the backlog (4.5): 'never crashes, and we publish the number'; 'one file is the project'; 'the data your site needs, fetched for you'; 'free, open, runs on your Mac and in your browser'.
  • Channels: surveyor and civil subreddits and forums, land-development podcasts, permaculture and homesteading communities, state surveyor societies, GIS user groups; the CADCraft and ArtCraft communities.

4.7Roadmap, risks and metrics

Phases, with agent-hour estimates using the calibration the CADCraft project publishes (0.3–0.7 h per option or fix, 2–5 h per moderate feature, 15–100 h per subsystem, 1.3× verification tail). That calibration has proven optimistic for depth work, so the ranges are wide and the gates are what matter.

PhaseScopeGate to passEstimate
0 · FoundationPin the library crates at a tagged CADCraft release and fork engine and ui-egui; lint wall and CI gate, project container and manifest, reference engine with cache and health, GUIDs, 3D points and tolerance context, native format, real-file corpus with design partnersA packaged project round-trips between two machines with zero broken links; corpus green in CI150–250 h
1 · Ground truthPoints and survey import, COGO and legal descriptions, parcels and plats, TIN surfaces from points/DEM/LandXML, contours, volumes, coordinate systems and basemaps, feeds: USGS, parcelsA boundary and topo from a real survey matches the surveyor's map check200–300 h
2 · Design the siteSetbacks and rule checks, pads and feature-line grading, cut/fill, walls, alignments and profiles for driveways and small streets, parking and ADA routesA graded site plan with balanced earthwork for a design partner250–400 h
3 · WaterCatchments, rainfall feed, runoff, pipes and structures, inlets and culverts, detention and bioretention, swales, SWPPP sheet, utilities and septicA drainage report and utility plan accepted by a reviewer250–400 h
4 · Plans and permitsLabel styles, layer standards, sheets and view frames, quantities, submittal package, as-built compare, stakeout exportThe first permit-ready plan set accepted by a jurisdiction150–250 h
5 · Old-school and neoSolar arrays with shading and yield, rainwater harvesting, greywater, passive-solar siting, earth and timber wall types with quantities, defensible space, flood resilienceA homestead site plan with energy and water budgets150–250 h
6 · ShareHosted sync, review links, plug-ins (Rust/WASM), jurisdiction packs, LISP migration helpersTen paying teams200–350 h

Total: roughly 1,350–2,200 agent-hours, in the same range the CADCraft project estimates for its own remaining AutoCAD parity, but spent on a product a site designer can finish a project with at the end of phase 4.

Risks

RiskImpactMitigation
Civil 3D objects have no public specificationImported Civil 3D drawings arrive as proxy geometryExchange through LandXML, which Civil 3D exports natively; accept geometry plus sidecar data; never promise Civil 3D object fidelity
DWG fidelity through the third-party bridgeLossy exchange damages trustNative format first; unknown data opaque; corpus and an external validator in CI; contribute fixes upstream to acadrust
Jurisdictions differRule checks wrong somewhereRule sheets are data with provenance and a reviewer sign-off; start with design-partner jurisdictions only
Scope creep toward Civil 3D parityNever finishedThe feature set above is the scope; corridors stay templates; a change needs a design-partner request
Public data licences and availabilityFeeds break or cannot be redistributedCache snapshots in the project with provenance; abstract providers; public-domain sources first
Estimates are optimisticPhases slipGates, not dates; publish measured progress the way CADCraft's parity docs do
Trademarks and clean-roomLegal exposureNo Autodesk/Bricsys assets, no reading GPL CAD sources, nominative use only, own name and brand
Upstream API churn in the CADCraft cratesEach CADCraft release breaks the Genesis buildPin crate versions, upgrade on a schedule with the corpus as the gate, and send API-stabilising changes upstream rather than patching locally
The DWG bridge is a one-maintainer crate (acadrust)DWG support stalls or the licence changesVendor the pinned version, fund or co-maintain it, keep DXF as the guaranteed exchange path, and never depend on DWG for the native format

Success metrics

  • Crashes in the field: 0, published monthly with the opt-in reporter's totals.
  • Time from survey to permit-ready plan set for a one-acre commercial site: under two working days for a trained drafter.
  • Warm re-reference of a 50 MB base: under one second; cold open of a 10 MB DWG: under ten seconds.
  • Real-file corpus: 100% of projects round-trip (native) and 100% of DWG exports open in two other CAD programs without loss of geometry or text.
  • Design partners: five firms producing real deliverables by the end of phase 4.

A.2 Fact-check verdicts on the research claims

Each research worker nominated its key claims; independent checkers tried to refute each one with other sources. 54 claims were checked and 4 were refuted or could not be verified; every refutation was carried into the page, and the correction column holds the wording now used. “Refuted” means contradicted or unverifiable, not necessarily false.

WorkerClaimVerdictCorrection / precise wordingEvidence
inventory:cadcraftCADCraft is pre-alpha; 4 of its 5 core AutoCAD workflows fail its own alpha gate (precise drafting, blocks, plotting, lossless DWG save), only dimensioning passes.holds highCorrect, with these precisions: (1) It is the project's self-assessment, not an independent test. The upstream storytold/cadcraft ROADMAP.md (last updated 2026-10-14; still current on main as of 2026-10-21, after the v0.10.0 release) says "Stage: pre-alpha" and "four of AutoCAD's five core workflows fail the alpha gate"; docs/roadmap.md#alpha-gate (revised 2026-10-12) lists the five rows with "Works end to end?" statuses: draw/modify precisely with object snaps and tracking = "partial, blocking"; use blocks = "partial, blocking"; layers/layouts/plotting a sheet = "partial, blocking"; save DWG that AutoCAD opens without loss = "no"; dimension and annotate = "partial, not blocking" (so dimensioning "passes" the gate but is itself only partial: no DIMSTYLE dialog, MTEXT editor not in place). (2) The gate is the craftrules progress-docs core-workflow gate shared across the ArtCraft apps, applied in CADCraft's own docs; a second stated reason for pre-alpha is "ready for real work ≈ 38%", under the ~40% alpha bar; alpha is estimated ≈ 125–190 h away. (3) The ROADMAP.md banner's reasons are partly stale versus docs/roadmap.md: the banner says blocks "can't be edited in a block editor" and "plots ignore plot styles", while docs/roadmap.md (2026-10-12) records that the Block Editor (BEDIT/BSAVE/BCLOSE), xrefs, plot styles (CTB/STB), Plot dialog/preview and PUBLISH have landed, with REFEDIT, XCLIP, dynamic blocks and direct-to-printer output still missing; the row statuses and hours were explicitly left "unchanged" (still blocking). (4) Third-party coverage (e.g. pasqualepillitteri.it) merely repeats the roadmap; one mirror site reportedly labels v0.7.0 "Alpha", but that is not the project's own label. inventory:cadcraft-refsAn xref is a Block with xref_path saved exactly as given; its objects are loaded from the referenced file's model space at open and on Reload and never saved with the host; xref_status is session-onlyholds highCorrect, with these precisions: (1) The path is stored verbatim (only trimmed of surrounding whitespace by `-xref path`) in `Block::xref_path` and written unchanged into the BLOCK record's group 1 (flag 4, plus 8 for an overlay); the DXF reader stores group 1 verbatim and ignores any entities inside a BLOCK flagged 4. (2) Objects are the referenced drawing's model space (`x.model`, adopted with fresh handles, references renamed to `xref|name`, dimension associativity cleared; its INSBASE/INSUNITS become the block's base/units; its blocks come along as `xref|name`). They are loaded not only at OPEN (`after_open` -> `load_all`) and `-xref Reload`, but also at XATTACH, `-xref Attach/Overlay` and `-xref Path`. (3) 'Never saved' holds for both DXF and DWG: `saved_view` empties every block that has an `xref_path` and is applied inside `tags_for`, which both the DXF writer (`tags`) and the DWG bridge (`dwg_bridge`) use. (4) `xref_status` is never written to DXF/DWG (neither format has a field for it; `mark_xrefs` writes only flags and path), reads back as the default `NotLoaded`, and is recomputed by `after_open`, so an Unloaded xref is reloaded on reopen (docs/gaps.md F2: 'Unloaded state and xref-layer overrides last only for the session'). Strictly, the field is `#[serde(default)]` rather than `#[serde(skip)]`, so a serde serialization of `Block` would include it; 'session-only' means 'not persisted in any drawing file', not 'excluded from serde'.Cited sources verified verbatim: /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/doc/src/tables.rs lines 661-708 (Block.xref_path, xref_overlay, xref_status doc comments 'session state; not saved'); /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/io/src/dxf_xref.rs saved_view; /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/cmd/blocks/xref.rs module doc. inventory:cadcraft-refsStored paths are followed only on this computer or the drawing's own network share; UNC paths to other hosts, file://host URLs, device paths and URL schemes are refused with a reason and treated like a missing fileholds highThe claim is correct in substance; precise wording: Paths stored in a drawing (xref paths and table data links) are resolved only through craft_docpath (pinned at craft-libs rev 82a24b0 in /home/user/cadcraft_1/Cargo.toml), a lexical, no-I/O check. A relative stored path is taken from the drawing's folder; a UNC path is followed only when its host and share equal the drawing's own share (compared ignoring ASCII case), otherwise it is refused as Refused::Network ("the path is on another computer"). file: URLs with a non-local host (anything but an empty host or "localhost") are refused as Network, while file:///path, file://localhost/path and file:///C:/... are accepted as local paths. Device paths (\\.\, \\?\ non-drive, \??\, and DOS reserved names such as CON, NUL, COM1 with or without extension) are refused as Refused::Device ("the path names a device, not a file"); non-file URL schemes (http, https, smb, webdav, mailto, data, javascript, ms-word, ...) are refused as Refused::Url ("the link is not a file path"); empty/control-character/above-root paths, and relative paths when the drawing has no file, are Refused::Invalid. Consequences: an xref with a refused path is not loaded, its block stays empty, and its status is XrefStatus::Unresolved (not NotFound) with the log line "<path>: not opened, <reason>"; -XREF ? shows no foundAt, XOPEN fails with the reason, and reload follows the saved path the same way. As with a genuinely missing file, the loader still looks for a file of the same name beside the host drawing, so a same-named local copy can satisfy a refused xref path. A refused table data link is reported as a per-link error "<path>: not opened, <reason>" and nothing is read (no fallback). Paths the user types (XATTACH, -XREF Path, DATALINK) are used as given. The behaviour is documented only in code comments and the craft-libs README; no cadcraft docs/ page mentions it, and the craftrules standards repo could not be checked (not accessible from this session).Independent sources: (1) craft-libs README, github.com/storytold/craft-libs — "Paths stored in documents are followed only when they are local or on the document's own network share; other network paths, device paths and non-file URLs are refused." (2) craft-libs current lib.rs, raw.githubusercontent.com/storytold/craft-lib… — Refused variants Network/Device/Url/Invalid with Display reasons; same-share UNC allowed case-insensitively, other hosts Network; file://host Network, empty host/localhost local; http/https etc. Url; \\.\, \\?\, \??\, CON/NUL/COM1 Device; lexical, no I/O. (3) craft-libs integration tests, raw.githubusercontent.com/storytold/craft-lib… — asserts those classifications (\\h\s, //h/s, \\?\UNC\, file://h/ -> Network; \\.\C:, \\.\pipe, \??\, CON, nul.tar.gz, COM1, NUL: -> Device; http:, https:, smb:, webdav:, mailto:, data:, javascript:, ms-word: -> Url; file:/// and file://localhost/... local; same-share UNC resolves with case-insensitive share match). (4) The cited pinned-rev source, raw.githubusercontent.com/storytold/craft-lib… — same semantics. Local corroboration: /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/doc_path.rs (wrapper; unit test passes: `cargo test -p cadcraft-engine --lib doc_path` -> 1 passed); /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/cmd/blocks/xref/load.rs lines 144-217 (Missing::Refused -> XrefStatus::Unresolved, format "{path}: not opened, {r}"; same-name fallback beside host); /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/cmd/table/breaks.rs lines 228-234 (data link error "{path}: not opened, {r}"); /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/tests/xref.rs lines 461-505 and /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/tests/table_breaks.rs lines 144-171 (assert "another computer" in log/errors, Unresolved status, user-given path used as given); /home/user/cadcraft_1/Cargo.toml line 28 (craft-docpath pinned to rev 82a24b0). Not verifiable: github.com/storytold/craftrules returned 404 and gh API access was not enabled for storytold repos in this session.
inventory:cadcraft-refsHost overrides on xref layers survive save and reopen in both DXF and DWGholds highCorrect, with two precisions. (1) Scope: what is proven is that colour and freeze overrides set in the host on an xref's layers (`xref|layer`) are written as xref-dependent LAYER records (DXF group 70 bits 16|32, with the linetypes they use) and come back after save and reopen in both DXF and DWG, mirroring AutoCAD's VISRETAIN=1 default. The whole LAYER record is written, so other settings travel too, but only colour and frozen are asserted by tests, and the `$VISRETAIN` header variable itself is still not written (PR #889). (2) Provenance: this landed upstream in storytold/cadcraft PR #889 "Save xref layer overrides with the host, in DXF and DWG" (merged 2026-10-13, fixing issue #886); before it the overrides were lost on save. Note that docs/gaps.md row F2 (locally and on upstream main) still says "Unloaded state and xref-layer overrides last only for the session" — that sentence is stale and contradicted by the code, both tests and the merged PR.Independent sources beyond the cited test: (a) Upstream issue github.com/storytold/cadcraft/issues/886 ("Saving drops xref layer overrides: host colour/freeze on xref|layer revert to the xref's own values on reopen (DXF and DWG)", opened 2026-10-13) closed as completed by merged PR github.com/storytold/cadcraft/pull/889 ("Save xref layer overrides with the host, in DXF and DWG", merged 2026-10-13, commit 69f2b97; notes $VISRETAIN header is still not written). (b) Autodesk VISRETAIN documentation help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/PTB/?guid=GUI… — value 1 (initial): "Xref-dependent layer changes made in the current drawing take precedence. inventory:cadcraft-refsA missing xref leaves an empty block with status Not Found, the host opens, and the reference survives a save; a file later placed beside the host is found by nameholds highCorrect, with two precisions. (1) The file placed beside the host is picked up on the next load attempt (-XREF Path/Reload, or reopening the host), not spontaneously while the drawing sits open; the cited test triggers it with `-xref` option "path" pointing at a stale Windows path, and the lookup falls back to the file name in the host's folder. (2) "Not Found" is the status only when the file simply does not exist; in CADCraft a saved path that the path policy refuses (and no same-named file beside the host) yields "Unresolved" instead. CADCraft's fallback search is narrower than AutoCAD's (saved path, then host folder; AutoCAD additionally tries project paths, support paths and the shortcut's Start In folder).1) Ran the cited test in-repo: `cargo test -p cadcraft-engine --test xref missing_files_leave_the_host_intact` -> "test missing_files_leave_the_host_intact ... ok" (/home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/tests/xref.rs lines 218-241). It asserts: after `open`, block "gone" has status XrefStatus::NotFound with 0 entities (empty block); the open command itself succeeds (helper `open` unwraps `s.execute("open", ...)`); the log contains "gone ... not loaded"; after `qsave`, the re-read file still has block "gone" with xref_path "gone.dxf"; after saving gone.dxf beside host.dxf and running `-xref` path, status becomes Loaded. 2) Engine implementation /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/cmd/blocks/xref/load.rs: `candidates()` documents the search as "the path itself (a relative one against the host's folder), then its file name in the host's folder"; `Missing::NotFound` maps to `XrefStatus::NotFound` with message "file not found"; `Missing::Refused` maps to `XrefStatus::Unresolved`. Status label "Not Found" defined in /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/doc/src/tables.rs (`XrefStatus::label`). 3) Independent vendor documentation, obtained through the Autodesk Product Help connector (direct fetch of help.autodesk.com was blocked by the proxy): "About Missing or Circular External References" help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2024/ENU/?guid=GUI… — states the program stores the xref path, on open/plot/reload it checks it, a missing file produces an error message and placeholder text at each insertion, a task dialog lets you "ignore all missing xrefs or update their folder locations" (i.e. the host still opens), and the search order when the original path fails is: "Current folder of the host drawing", then project search paths (PROJECTNAME), support search paths, then the shortcut's Start In folder. 4) "List View / Tree View Pane (External References Palette)" help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2024/CHT/?guid=GUI… (same GUID also at help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2023/ENU/AutoCAD-…) — defines the status values: "Not Found - The referenced file no longer exists in the valid search paths", "Unresolved - The referenced file cannot be read", and "Saved Path - Displays the path that is saved with the drawing when the referenced file is attached". 5) Web search result corroborating the "Not Found" status meaning from a third party: landfx.com/kb/autocad-fxcad/images/2561-missi… (fetch blocked; search summary only).
inventory:cadcraft-refsNested xrefs load to at most 16 levels, overlays are not nested, circular references are detected by a canonical-path chain, and loads are capped at 4M objects / 1000 files / 512 MiB per file / 1 GiB totalholds highCorrect; precise wording: (1) The depth bound is not an xref-specific constant but the shared block-nesting bound `MAX_BLOCK_DEPTH = 16` defined in crates/doc/src/extents.rs ("Nested block references deeper than this are ignored (cyclic or hostile files)"). `fetch` in load.rs refuses with "xrefs are nested too deeply" when `depth >= MAX_BLOCK_DEPTH`, where the host drawing's own xrefs are depth 0, so up to 16 referenced drawings deep load below the host and the 17th level is left Unresolved. (2) "Overlays are not nested" means: an overlay loads normally in the drawing that references it, but when that drawing is itself attached into another drawing, its overlays are skipped (the nested list is filtered with `!b.xref_overlay`); only attached xrefs propagate. This mirrors AutoCAD's documented behaviour ("Unlike blocks and attached xrefs, overlaid xrefs cannot be nested"). (3) Circular detection: `Ctx.chain` is a Vec<PathBuf> of `std::fs::canonicalize`d paths (falling back to the path as given if canonicalisation fails; identity on wasm), seeded with the host drawing's own path; a candidate whose canonical path is already in the chain is refused as "circular reference"; the chain is pushed before and popped after loading a file's nested xrefs, so only ancestors currently being loaded count (the same file reached twice via a diamond is loaded twice, not refused). (4) Caps: `MAX_XREF_OBJECTS = 4_000_000` (cumulative count of each referenced drawing's model-space plus block entities; refused when the running total exceeds 4,000,000), `MAX_XREF_FILES = 1000` (the 1001st file read is refused), `MAX_XREF_BYTES = 512 << 20` (512 MiB) per file and `MAX_XREF_TOTAL_BYTES = 1 << 30` (1 GiB) cumulative. The caps are per load call (one `Ctx`): all xrefs of a drawing together at OPEN (`load_all`), or the named set for XATTACH / -XREF Reload / Path. The two byte caps exist only in non-wasm builds; on wasm no referenced file is read at all ("files are not available on the web"). The user-facing messages print the byte limits as "MB" although the values are MiB. A cap or depth failure leaves that one xref empty with status Unresolved and a reason, and never stops the host drawing from opening.Cited source verified locally: /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/cmd/blocks/xref/load.rs (module doc lines 8-11; consts at lines 20-29; `fetch` lines 318-344 with the depth, chain, file-count checks and the `!b.xref_overlay` nested filter; `read` lines 221-254 byte caps; `import` lines 350-354 object cap). Independent sources: (a) /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/doc/src/extents.rs line 10 `pub const MAX_BLOCK_DEPTH: usize = 16;` (external copy: raw.githubusercontent.com/Sparkey333/cadcraft…). (b) Integration tests /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/tests/xref.rs `overlays_do_not_load_in_the_drawings_that_reference_their_host` (asserts an overlay loads in its own host, `objects(&t, "plan|site") == 0` when that host is attached elsewhere, and 2 after switching it to attach) and `circular_and_deeply_nested_references_are_bounded` (a->b->a leaves `b|a` empty; a 20-file chain stops within MAX_BLOCK_DEPTH); both executed in this session with `cargo test -p cadcraft-engine --test xref` and passed (2 passed, 0 failed). External copy: raw.githubusercontent.com/Sparkey333/cadcraft…. (c) Public mirror of the cited file, fetched and matching verbatim: raw.githubusercontent.com/Sparkey333/cadcraft…. (d) Project knowledge pack restating the same limits (derived from the same file, so not independent): /home/user/cadcraft_1/docs/genesis/ai/knowledge/cadcraft-facts.md line 209 and docs/genesis/ai/training/facts.jsonl line 115 ("Depth <= 16, cycle chain by canonical path, overlays not nested, file/byte/object caps"). (e) Vendor documentation for the behaviour being mirrored: Autodesk -XREF (Command) help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/PTB/?guid=GUI… ("If you reference a drawing that contains an overlaid xref, the overlaid xref does not appear in the current drawing. Unlike blocks and attached xrefs, overlaid xrefs cannot be nested"); To Work With Overlays help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2027/ENU/?guid=GUI… ("Using the Overlay option excludes any nested xrefs"); About Missing or Circular External References help.autodesk.com/view/ACD/2024/ENU/?guid=GUI… (circular chain A>B>C>A is detected and broken at load). Web search (novedge.com/blogs/design-news/autocad-tip-man…, cadforum.cz/en/qaID.asp?tip=4155) found no published CADCraft page stating these numeric caps; they are documented only in code comments, constants and tests.
inventory:cadcraft-refsTable data links read CSV/TSV only (8 MiB), with no Excel, no write-back and no web support, and travel as CADCraft xdata rather than AutoCAD DATALINK objectsholds highCorrect; slightly more precise wording: CADCraft's DATALINK reads delimited text only — .csv, plus .txt/.tsv as tab-separated (semicolon-delimited CSV is auto-detected) — capped at 8 MiB (MAX_LINK_BYTES = 8 << 20, reported to the user as "8 MB"); no XLS/XLSX, no sheet/named-range/cell-range selection, no DATALINKUPDATE write-back to the source file, and the wasm build returns the error "data links can't read files in the browser". The link (path + start row) is saved as JSON under the TABLEX marker in CADCRAFT registered-app xdata on the ACAD_TABLE entity, not as an AcDbDataLink object in the drawing's ACAD_DATALINK dictionary, so other CAD programs do not see it as a data link.Local repo (fork of storytold/cadcraft): /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/engine/src/cmd/table/breaks.rs (line 15 `const MAX_LINK_BYTES: u64 = 8 << 20;`, line 27 params `path (.csv, .txt/.tsv tab-separated)`, lines 237-254 read_link with wasm32 branch `"data links can't read files in the browser"`; no xls/xlsx/write-back code), /home/user/cadcraft_1/crates/io/src/dxf_table.rs (TableExtra.link serialized to JSON xdata under TABLEX marker; APP = "CADCRAFT" in crates/io/src/dxf_ext.rs; grep of crates/ finds no ACAD_DATALINK/AcDbDataLink writer), /home/user/cadcraft_1/docs/gaps.md row F11. research:civil3d-featuresCivil 3D 2027/2027.1 uses the AutoCAD 2018 drawing format; drawings open in Civil 3D 2019–2023 and 2018, but AECC objects are not backward compatible and show as proxies with Civil 3D commands disabled in older releasesholds highCorrect as a quotation of Autodesk's docs, but imprecise in two places. Precise wording: (1) Civil 3D 2027 and 2027.1 use the AutoCAD 2018 DWG format (AC1032); Autodesk's AutoCAD file-format table lists "AutoCAD 2018 - AutoCAD 2027 -> native format AutoCAD 2018". (2) The 2027/2027.1 release notes say drawings "can be opened in Autodesk Civil 3D versions 2019 through 2023, and AutoCAD Civil 3D 2018" (unchanged boilerplate), but the 2027 "Interoperability Between Versions" topic and Autodesk's Civil 3D Compatibility Matrix state the broader, accurate range: 2027 drawings can be opened AND EDITED in Civil 3D 2018 through the current version (2018+), i.e. 2024-2026 are included. (3) The sentence "the AECC objects in each release are not backward compatible... displayed as proxy objects, and all Autodesk Civil 3D commands are disabled" is generic text in the Drawing Compatibility topic; in practice it applies when the Civil 3D object format changes (last at 2018; earlier at 2013/2017) or when a 2027 drawing is saved to a pre-2018 format. Since 2018 the object format has been compatible, and only newly introduced object types degrade in older releases (e.g. 2021 rail bands/bridge objects and 2023 corridor transitions as proxy graphics/view-only). For 2027 specifically, the new drainage objects (ponds, channels, underground storage) stay visible and non-proxy in older releases with read-only geometry via on-demand upgrades. Pre-2018 releases need PROXYGRAPHICS=1 + Save As to an older format (objects become proxy graphics), or LandXML export/import to edit.Autodesk 2027.1 release notes (via Autodesk Product Help search index): help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=c… — "Autodesk Civil 3D 2027.1 uses the AutoCAD 2018 drawing format. Drawings saved in Autodesk Civil 3D 2027.1 can be opened in Autodesk Civil 3D versions 2019 through 2023, and AutoCAD Civil 3D 2018." Identical wording for 2027 at help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=c…. Drawing Compatibility topic help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=G… — "the AECC objects in each release are not backward compatible. When a drawing is opened in a prior release, AEC objects are displayed as proxy objects, and all Autodesk Civil 3D commands are disabled." Independent Autodesk sources: Civil 3D Compatibility Matrix autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s… — row "Civil 3D 2027 | AutoCAD 2018 | Drawings can be opened and edited in Civil 3D 2018+ | See Drainage Tools compatibility"; AutoCAD file-format table autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s… — "AutoCAD 2018 - AutoCAD 2027 | AutoCAD 2018"; 2027 Interoperability Between Versions help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=G… — "Drawings saved in Autodesk Civil 3D 2027 can be opened in Autodesk Civil 3D 2018 through the current version"; On-Demand Object Upgrades help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=G… — drainage objects in older versions are "visible and not proxy", geometry read-only. research:civil3d-featuresData shortcuts commonly break when the working folder or drawings are moved/renamed, network connection drops at startup, permissions differ, or paths exceed limits; Autodesk's guidance is to never rename or move referenced filesholds highSubstantively correct, with two precision fixes. (1) The cited Autodesk support article lists these causes verbatim: "Wrong location of Working Folder has been set up or moved: Possible minor changes such as a moved or renamed drawing. Possible minor changes of renaming or moved data shortcut working folder. Connection to the network folder containing source data is not available while Civil 3D starts up or is intermittent. Files and shortcuts are saved on a Non-Microsoft Network. Missing updates for Civil 3D and Desktop Connector. Missing working folder paths of server or cloud location." Its solutions include "Check Folder Permission". It does NOT mention path length; that cause comes from sibling Autodesk articles: "Data Shortcuts are missing from the Prospector" (verify "the path lengths do not exceed Window's file path length limit"), "The data shortcut XML file could not be updated" ("The Windows file path length limit has been exceeded... Ensure the XML file path does not exceed the character limit of 255"), and the Forma/ACC migration best practices (Desktop Connector cannot handle paths "greater than 244 characters"). Permission mismatches between users are documented for BIM 360/ACC projects ("Insufficient permissions to the folder two levels above the Data Shortcuts project folder... making it unavailable for others with different permissions"). (2) "Never rename or move" overstates Autodesk's wording. Civil 3D Help "Best Practices" says: "To minimize broken references, do not move or rename shared objects or their source drawings after creation. You can move an entire working folder. If you need to move or rename multiple objects or data shortcuts in a working folder, use the stand-alone Data Shortcuts Editor... In a network environment, use UNC paths." The "never rename or move" phrasing is closer to the WisDOT knowledge base (a state DOT, not Autodesk): "It is generally recommended that civil objects and files are NOT renamed or moved after they have been data referenced in other files." Suggested wording: "Autodesk's documented causes of broken data shortcuts include a moved/renamed drawing or working folder, the network folder being unavailable or intermittent while Civil 3D starts, insufficient or mismatched folder permissions, and (per related Autodesk articles) paths exceeding the Windows limit (~255) or Desktop Connector's 244-character limit; Autodesk's best practice is 'do not move or rename shared objects or their source drawings after creation' (moving the entire working folder is allowed; use the Data Shortcuts Editor for bulk repathing and UNC paths on networks)."Cited Autodesk support article (full body retrieved via Autodesk's official Product Help knowledge server, since autodesk.com is blocked by this session's proxy): autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s… — lists moved/renamed drawing or working folder, network folder unavailable/intermittent at startup, non-Microsoft network, missing updates; solution "Check Folder Permission". Autodesk Civil 3D Help "Best Practices": help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2025/ENU/?guid=G… — "do not move or rename shared objects or their source drawings after creation. You can move an entire working folder... use the stand-alone Data Shortcuts Editor... use UNC paths". Path-length sources (Autodesk): autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s… ("path lengths do not exceed Window's file path length limit"); autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s… ("Windows file path length limit has been exceeded... character limit of 255... Check your permissions"); help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2025/ENU/?guid=G… ("Paths that are too long for Desktop Connector to handle (greater than 244 characters)"). Permission-mismatch source (Autodesk): autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s…. research:bricscad-featuresVertical alignments and corridors exist in BricsCAD civil tools and require projection; horizontal alignments import from LandXML and Civil 3Dholds highCorrect but incomplete. Precise wording: In BricsCAD Pro's Civil tools, vertical alignments (built by placing PVIs on the TIN-surface profile under a horizontal alignment, viewed with ALIGNMENTVIEW) and corridors (CORRIDOR command: a corridor template swept along a 3D alignment or string, or a 'simple corridor' along horizontal-alignment PI points on a TIN surface) both exist, and both require the horizontal alignment to first be projected (draped) onto a TIN surface ("Projection is required to create Vertical Alignments and Corridors"). Alignments can be imported two ways: LANDXMLIMPORT brings in Horizontal Alignments (by PI and by Elements), 3D Alignments, TIN surfaces, civil points and strings, and imported LandXML vertical alignments automatically create 3D Alignments; CIVIL3DIMPORT reads a Civil 3D DWG (or converts the current drawing) and imports Alignments (by PI and by Elements) AND Profiles (vertical alignments), plus COGO points, point groups, TIN/volume/grid surfaces and profile views. So it is not only horizontal alignments that import; vertical alignments/profiles do too. Minor caveats: LANDXMLIMPORT is documented as not available on Linux, and corridors themselves are not imported (they are rebuilt in BricsCAD from a template plus 3D alignment).Direct fetches of help.bricsys.com, bricsys.com, forum.bricsys.com and reseller mirrors were blocked by the session proxy (403 CONNECT / DNS), so all quotes come from search-engine snippets of these distinct pages. (1) Cited source, 'Creating alignments' guide (V22/V23 and current): help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/bricscad/civi… and help.bricsys.com/document/GD_creatingalignmen… — "The Horizontal Alignment is not necessarily always projected (draped) on the TIN Surface. Projection is required to create Vertical Alignments and Corridors." and "By defining Points of Vertical Intersection (PVI) on these vertical profiles of the target surfaces, the new 2D curve defines the vertical alignment." (2) CORRIDOR command reference: help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/command-refer… — "Creates a corridor from a corridor template. There are two methods for creating a corridor: 1. Creates a corridor using a corridor template along an 3D alignment line or a string..." (second method: simple corridor along horizontal alignment PI points on a TIN surface). Also 'Modeling a Corridor': help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/bricscad/civi…. (3) ALIGNMENTVIEW reference: help.bricsys.com/document/_commandreference--… — "if the horizontal alignment is in a drawing that is not projected yet on TIN surface, then TIN surface needs to be selected in the next step." (4) LANDXMLIMPORT reference: help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/command-refer… — "The following BricsCAD Civil entities can be imported from the specified LandXML file: Civil Points, TIN Surfaces, Horizontal Alignments (both Alignments by PI and Alignments by Elements are supported), 3D Alignments, and Strings." and "Imported vertical alignments automatically create 3D Alignments." plus "This command is not currently available on Linux." (5) CIVIL3DIMPORT reference (V22 and current): help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/command-refer… and help.bricsys.com/document/_commandreference--… — importable objects include TIN Surfaces, TIN Volume Surfaces, Grid Surfaces, "Alignments (both Alignments by PI and Alignments by Elements are supported)", "Profiles (Vertical Alignments)", COGO points, point groups and profile views. (6) Bricsys V21 civil blog: bricsys.com/blog/whats-new-for-civil-design-i… (mirror bricscad.octave.com/blog/whats-new-for-civil-…) — "The new CIVIL3DIMPORT command enables you to create multiple civil entities in your BricsCAD drawing, from an external Autodesk Civil 3D drawing, in one operation." and "Horizontal alignments now support spirals and vertical alignments now support parabolic curves." (7) Third-party corroboration that the workflow exists (search snippets only): YouTube 'Civil Design - 60 min BricsCAD Pro In Depth Session' youtube.com/watch?v=9slBsZhlFgk (covers TIN surfaces, vertical alignment creation, corridors) and BricsCAD forum thread forum.bricsys.com/discussion/40489/help-super….
research:bricscad-featuresBricsCAD Pro includes civil tools (TIN, grading, alignments) and point-cloud processing formerly sold as add-onsholds highBricsCAD Pro includes Bricsys's civil/survey tools (TIN surface creation and editing, grading, alignments, and since V24 corridors/the Civil & Survey toolkit) and native point-cloud processing (attach/pre-process, crop, classify, TIN-from-point-cloud, linework tracing). These were not sold as add-ons: through V20 they were restricted to the higher-priced BricsCAD Platinum edition, and when Bricsys discontinued Platinum with V21 (2020) all Platinum features were merged into Pro. (Third-party point-cloud plugins such as PointCab 4Brics remain separately sold add-ons; some point-cloud feature-recognition tools, e.g. Scan-to-BIM, are BIM-edition only.)Pro includes civil + point cloud tools: Bricsys blog bricsys.com/blog/bricscad-pro-v24-civil-and-s… ; Bricsys blog bricsys.com/blog/v23-2-features-in-bricscad-p… (TIN from point cloud, DENSIFY in Pro Civil) ; BricsCAD Pro V25 datasheet webobjects2.cdw.com/is/content/CDW/cdw/on-dom… ("civil design tools for creating and editing TIN surfaces, grading and alignments... corridor editing"; "point-cloud visualization", "automatic point-cloud classifier") ; Bricsys V26 product guide cdn.prod.website-files.com/67857378605ed176b8… (Pro adds civil and survey tools, point clouds; BIM adds point cloud feature recognition) ; Bricsys help help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/bricscad/civi… and help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/command-refer… ; thatcadgirl.com/faq/what-can-i-do-with-point-… (V26 Pro point cloud commands; POINTCLOUDATTACH requires Pro). research:bricscad-reviewsV25.1.07 users got fatal errors while panning/zooming; Bricsys support's workaround was MTFLAGS=0.holds mediumCorrect in substance, but imprecise. Precise wording: In a January 2025 Bricsys forum thread ("Panning and crashing"), a user running BricsCAD V25.1.07-1 (Civil/survey workflow, small drawing of survey points, linework and a few blocks) reported a fatal error while panning or zooming. That user relayed that Bricsys support (via a support request, not a staff post in the thread) asked them to set MTFLAGS=0 and test whether the issue still occurred; the user then reported that this fixed their crashing problem. A second user on the same build reported black-screen crashes when panning/zooming on an AMD Pro WX3200 / Ryzen 5600 system and said support had not been able to help them, so MTFLAGS=0 was a diagnostic step that resolved one reported case, not a universally confirmed or officially documented fix. MTFLAGS is Bricsys's bit-coded multithreading flag (default 3015; 0 = no parallel processing of display and loading). V25.1.07 shipped 16 Dec 2024 and its release notes contain no pan/zoom crash fix; a related but narrower crash ("crash when panning while drawing annotative dimensions") was fixed earlier in V25.1.06.Primary thread (content confirmed via four independent search-index readings; direct fetch blocked by the session proxy): forum.bricsys.com/discussion/39491/panning-an… and comment permalink forum.bricsys.com/discussion/comment/55411/ — OP on V25.1.07-1-en_us, fatal error panning/zooming; "Bricsys support asked them to set MTFLAGS=0 and test if the issue still occurs"; later "this has fixed my crashing problem"; second user (AMD WX3200) said support had not helped. Corroborating sources: MTFLAGS reference (bit flags for parallel processing of display and loading, 0 = none, default 3015): help.bricsys.com/en-us/document/system-variab… and help.bricsys.com/document/_systemvariablerefe… ; Microvellum KB on MTFLAGS default 3015 / multithreading issues with third-party apps: intercom.help/microvellum-knowledge-base/en/a… ; other threads where Bricsys support or users tied crashes to MTFLAGS / suggested MTFLAGS 0: forum.bricsys.com/discussion/38282/stability-… and forum.bricsys.com/discussion/34244/crashing ; V25.1.07 release notes (dated 16.12.2024, no pan/zoom crash fix) boa.bricscad.octave.com/common/releasenotes.j… and boa.bricsys.com/common/releasenotes.jsp ; V25.1.07 release date 12/16/2024: adaptiva.com/patch-library/1000000787_patch_f… ; V25.1.06 annotative-dimension pan crash fix: boa.bricsys.com/common/releasenotes.jsp?i=9051

research:opensource-reviewsQCAD Professional costs roughly $50 one-time with one year of updates; the Community Edition lacks DWG, polyline and viewport features, which are Pro plugins.holds highQCAD Professional is listed on the vendor's own shop at $48.00 USD (EUR 41 ex-tax; qcad.ch lists EUR 40 download / EUR 60 with USB stick) as a perpetual single-user license with one year of update access included; further years can be bought at a 30% discount, and the license itself never expires. QCAD Professional is the GPLv3 Community Edition plus commercial, proprietary plugins. Those plugins provide DWG read/write (via ODA Teigha libraries; CE is DXF-only), most polyline editing tools (add/delete nodes, trim, offset, polyline-from-selection, per-segment widths; CE still has basic line/arc-segment polylines, so CE lacks the Pro polyline tools rather than polylines altogether), and paper-space layout blocks and viewports (a QCAD representative: "layout blocks and viewports are only supported in QCAD Professional"; CE's "Viewports" preference only splits the on-screen model view). The 'CE + plugins for DWG, polylines and viewports' wording in the cited thread 9717 is a forum user's summary, not an official statement, though it matches the vendor's license page, features table and forum answers.Vendor shop page (price $48.00 USD / EUR 41 ex-tax, perpetual single-user license, one year of updates, renewal at 30% discount): qcad.org/prestashop/en/qcad/20-qcad-professio… ; vendor Swiss shop (EUR 40 download, EUR 60 with USB, one year free updates, licenses not time limited): qcad.ch/online-shop?cur=USD ; vendor license page (Professional = Community Edition + commercial proprietary plugins for DWG support, improved DXF, improved spline and polyline support): qcad.org/en/license ; vendor features table (DWG read/write and most polyline tools marked Pro/CAM-only; CE has basic line/arc-segment polylines): qcad.org/en/features/ ; vendor forum, QCAD representative, 2021 ("Yes, layout blocks and viewports are only supported in QCAD Professional"): forum.qcad.org/t/paper-space-requirements/7045 ; vendor changelog ("Viewport > Add Viewport" under Professional section): qcad.org/en/changelog/ ; Add Viewport reference under the Pro script tree: qcad.org/doc/qcad/latest/reference/en/scripts… ; forum "Feature check between CE and Pro versions" pointer to the features table: qcad.org/rsforum/viewtopic.php?f=76&t=4390 ; forum post on CE "Viewports" preference being a model-view split, not paper-space viewports: qcad.org/rsforum/viewtopic.php?t=11407 ; Wikipedia (DWG via proprietary plugin, CE distributed as source only since 3.7): en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QCAD ; third-party price corroboration (~$50 USD one-time): cadsoftusa.com/blog/qcad-vs-librecad-which-on… ; the researcher's cited threads (user-level summary of Pro = CE + plugins for DWG, polylines, viewports; perpetual license, one-year download access): forum.qcad.org/t/pro-edition-and-new-communit… and forum.qcad.org/t/community-edition-vs-pro-edi…. research:landdev-domainUSGS 3DEP products are public domain and the TNM Access API returns tile lists for the 1 m DEM and LPC datasetsholds mediumCorrect but imprecise. Precise wording: USGS states "All 3DEP products are public domain" (U.S. Government public-domain label usa.gov/publicdomain/label/1.0; USGS requests credit but imposes no restriction). The National Map's TNM Access API (https://tnmaccess.nationalmap.gov/api/v1/products) takes a bbox or polygon plus the dataset tag (sbDatasetTag) "Digital Elevation Model (DEM) 1 meter" or "Lidar Point Cloud (LPC)" (with prodFormats=LAS,LAZ) and returns JSON with `total` and an `items[]` array, one entry per staged product/tile, each carrying `title`, `downloadURL`, `boundingBox`, `sizeInBytes` and `format` (1 m DEMs are staged as 10,000 x 10,000 m tiles; LPC items are per-tile LAS/LAZ files). Caveats: use `downloadURL` (the `downloadLazURL` field is typically null); the raw LAZ copy on AWS (s3://usgs-lidar) is Requester-Pays while the Entwine EPT copy (s3://usgs-lidar-public) is free; and the cited GRASS r.in.usgs addon supports only NED/NLCD/NAIP/LPC, not the 1 m DEM dataset, so it does not by itself support the 1 m DEM half of the claim (leafmap, coincident and USGS docs do). Live API behaviour was not exercised from this sandbox (nationalmap.gov blocked), so the "returns tile lists" part rests on USGS documentation and multiple maintained client codebases rather than a live response.Public domain (independent of the cited AWS registry): USGS data catalog, 1 m DEM collection: data.usgs.gov/datacatalog/data/USGS:77ae0551-… ; USGS data catalog, Lidar Point Cloud collection: data.usgs.gov/datacatalog/data/USGS%3Ab7e353d… ; data.gov harvests stating "All 3DEP products are public domain": catalog.data.gov/dataset/1-meter-digital-elev… and catalog.data.gov/dataset/lidar-point-cloud-us… ; AWS registry (cited; "US Government Public Domain"): registry.opendata.aws/usgs-lidar/ . TNM Access API dataset tags and items/downloadURL tile lists: uw-cryo/coincident download.py (tnmdataset "Digital Elevation Model (DEM) 1 meter"; _fetch_usgs_lpc_tiles with "Lidar Point Cloud (LPC)", prodFormats LAS,LAZ; reads item["downloadURL"]): raw.githubusercontent.com/uw-cryo/coincident/… ; opengeos/leafmap The_national_map_USGS wrapper (endpoint tnmaccess.nationalmap.gov/api/v1/products?, returns items[].downloadURL): raw.githubusercontent.com/opengeos/leafmap/ma… and notebook querying "Lidar Point Cloud (LPC)" with total/items: raw.githubusercontent.com/opengeos/leafmap/ma… ; OSGeo/grass-addons r.in.usgs (cited; same endpoint, LPC tag, no 1 m DEM dataset): raw.githubusercontent.com/OSGeo/grass-addons/… ; USGS FAQ that TNMAccess covers all TNM downloadable products: usgs.gov/faqs/how-do-i-download-national-map-… ; TNM Access API docs: apps.nationalmap.gov/tnmaccess/ ; 3DEP index noting 1 m DEM staged as 10,000 x 10,000 m tiles: index.nationalmap.gov/arcgis/rest/services/3D… . research:landdev-domainNOAA Atlas 14 point estimates are served by a CGI endpoint used in many public clients; Atlas 15 began as a 2024 Montana pilot with CONUS estimates in peer review in 2026holds mediumNOAA Atlas 14 point precipitation-frequency estimates are served by CGI scripts on hdsc.nws.noaa.gov: the officially documented text/CSV endpoint is https://hdsc.nws.noaa.gov/cgi-bin/hdsc/new/fe_text_mean.csv?lat=..&lon=..&data=depth|intensity&units=english|metric&series=pds|ams (plus fe_text_upper/lower/combined variants; many clients use the shorter /cgi-bin/new/ path), and a second script, /cgi-bin/new/cgi_readH5.py?...&type=pf, is what the PFDS web page itself and clients such as wepppy and the CRAN package gwavr call. GitHub code search finds roughly 44 files referencing fe_text_mean.csv (e.g. USEPA/SERTO, cheginit/swmmanywhere-us, DigitalWaterAnalytics/HydroAnalytics) and 49 referencing cgi_readH5.py (e.g. cran/gwavr, gpt-cmdr/ras-commander and hms-commander, USEPA/play-freeboard, ARS-SWRC/agwa, civicmapper/culvert-toolkit), and USGS pfdf (data.noaa.atlas14) and GRASS r.noaa.atlas14 wrap the same point query; note the cited wepppy repo uses cgi_readH5.py, not fe_text_mean.csv. NOAA Atlas 15 began with a pilot over Montana released September 26, 2024 (technical report v1.0.p), which was for comparison and feedback only and did not itself go through peer review; its review period is closed. For the contiguous U.S., NOAA's current schedule is "no earlier than September 2026" for preliminary (Volume 1 and 2) estimates released for peer review and feedback, with published CONUS estimates no earlier than September 2027 (Alaska, Hawaii and territories a year later); earlier NOAA timelines had said 2025/early 2026 and slipped after a July 2025 funding pause on Volume 2. research:landdev-domain2021 IRC has Appendices AS (straw bale), AR (light straw-clay) and AU (cob); 2024 IRC adds Appendix BL (hempcrete) as non-structural infill requiring local adoptionholds highThe claim is correct. More precise wording: the 2021 IRC contains Appendix AR "Light Straw-Clay Construction", Appendix AS "Strawbale Construction" and Appendix AU "Cob Construction (Monolithic Adobe)" (AU was new in 2021, based on code change RB299-19; the first printing accidentally omitted the "not mandatory unless specifically referenced in the adopting ordinance" line from AU's title, fixed in errata/second printing). The 2024 IRC adds new Appendix BL "Hemp-Lime (Hempcrete) Construction" (sections BL101-BL108), which defines hemp-lime as "a nonstructural, biocomposite insulation infill material composed of hemp hurd and a lime-based binder" and states "The provisions contained in this appendix are not mandatory unless specifically referenced in the adopting ordinance." Two nuances the claim omits: (1) the requirement for specific local adoption applies to every IRC appendix, including the 2021 AR/AS/AU ones, not only BL; (2) in the 2024 IRC the three 2021 appendices were relettered: AR became BI, AS became BJ, AU became BK (per the 2024 IRC preface relocation table), so a 2024-edition citation should use BI/BJ/BK/BL.ICC official code pages: codes.iccsafe.org/content/IRC2021P1/appendix-… (2021 IRC Appendix AU Cob Construction (Monolithic Adobe)); codes.iccsafe.org/content/THPOTIRC2021P1/appe… (Appendix AS Strawbale Construction, 2021); codes.iccsafe.org/s/IRC2024P1/appendix-bl-hem… (2024 IRC Appendix BL Hemp-Lime (Hempcrete) Construction; "nonstructural, biocomposite insulation infill material"; "not mandatory unless specifically referenced in the adopting ordinance"); codes.iccsafe.org/s/IRC2024P2/appendix-bj-str… (2024 relettering, BJ strawbale); codes.iccsafe.org/content/IRC2024V2.0/preface (relocation table AR->BI, AS->BJ, AU->BK, BL new). ICC printed tables of contents: shop.iccsafe.org/media/wysiwyg/material/3100S… (2021: AR Light Straw-Clay, AS Strawbale, AU Cob) and shop.iccsafe.org/media/wysiwyg/material/3100S… (2024: BI, BJ, BK, BL). research:landdev-domainPVWatts v8 accepts albedo, bifaciality, soiling, gcr and dc_ac_ratio inputsholds highCorrect, with these precisions: all five are optional request parameters of the PVWatts v8 API (GET /api/pvwatts/v8). dc_ac_ratio: decimal, default 1.2, must be positive. gcr: decimal, default 0.4, range 0.01-0.99. bifaciality: decimal, range 0-1, no default (ratio of rear- to front-side efficiency; typically 0.65-0.9). albedo: a single decimal or a pipe-delimited array of 12 monthly values, each >0 and <1, no default (a companion flag use_wf_albedo=0/1 selects weather-file albedo instead). soiling: a pipe-delimited array of 12 monthly values in percent, range 0-100, no default (new in v8 as the "monthly irradiance losses" input; bifaciality/albedo are also new relative to v6). Note the underlying SSC pvwattsv8 compute module has different internal defaults (dc_ac_ratio 1.1, gcr 0.3) than the web API's documented defaults (1.2, 0.4).Cited source (vendor doc source, confirms all five with types/defaults/ranges): github.com/NatLabRockies/developer.nlr.gov/bl… . Independent copy of the vendor doc source in the older NREL repo, same five rows plus the v8 change note ("bifacial module option, a new input for monthly irradiance losses" and new albedo inputs): github.com/NREL/developer.nrel.gov/blob/main/… . Independent code-level source, the SSC pvwattsv8 model the API runs, which declares SSC_INPUT entries "albedo" (Solar Resource, 1 constant or 12 monthly values), "bifaciality" (System Design, ?=0), "soiling" (System Design, %, array), "gcr" (System Design, ?=0.3, MIN=0.01,MAX=0.99) and "dc_ac_ratio" (System Design, ?=1.1, POSITIVE): github.com/NREL/ssc/blob/develop/ssc/cmod_pvw… . PySAM stub confirming the same attribute names and groups (albedo in SolarResource; bifaciality, soiling, gcr, dc_ac_ratio in SystemDesign): github.com/NREL/pysam/blob/main/stubs/stubs/P… . Rendered vendor pages developer.nrel.gov/docs/solar/pvwatts/v8/ and developer.nlr.gov/docs/solar/pvwatts/v8/ (and nrel-pysam.readthedocs.io/en/main/modules/Pvw…) were refused by this sandbox's egress proxy and could not be opened directly; web search summaries of those pages agreed with the above.
research:landdev-domain2010 ADA Standards Table 208.2 scopes accessible spaces by lot size and one in six accessible spaces must be van-accessibleholds highCorrect in substance, imprecise in wording. Precise version: 2010 ADA Standards section 208.2 / Table 208.2 ("Parking Spaces") sets the minimum number of accessible spaces based on the "Total Number of Parking Spaces Provided in Parking Facility" -- i.e. the count of spaces (not physical lot area), calculated separately for each parking facility (lot or structure), not on the site-wide total. Rows: 1-25 -> 1; 26-50 -> 2; 51-75 -> 3; 76-100 -> 4; 101-150 -> 5; 151-200 -> 6; 201-300 -> 7; 301-400 -> 8; 401-500 -> 9; 501-1000 -> 2 percent of total; 1001 and over -> 20 plus 1 for each 100, or fraction thereof, over 1000 (separate percentages apply to hospital outpatient 10%, rehab/outpatient PT 20%, and residential facilities under 208.2.1-208.2.3). Section 208.2.4 Van Parking Spaces: "For every six or fraction of six parking spaces required by 208.2 to comply with 502, at least one shall be a van parking space complying with 502" -- so the 1-in-6 ratio counts required accessible spaces (not total spaces), rounds up ("or fraction of six"), and always yields at least one van space. Note: DOJ's restriping brief table reads "500 to 1000" where the Standards say "501 to 1000".Primary text of the 2010 Standards (208.2, Table 208.2, Advisory 208.2, 208.2.4) read verbatim from DOJ's own ada.gov source repository: raw.githubusercontent.com/usdoj-crt/beta-ada/… (rendered at ada.gov/law-and-regs/design-standards/2010-st…; lines 1194-1277). Independent second copy from the U.S. Access Board's website source repository (ATBCB): raw.githubusercontent.com/atbcb/usab-uswds/ma… (rendered at access-board.gov/ada/chapter/ch02/), which carries identical Table 208.2 heading/rows and 208.2.4 wording. Further matching copies found via GitHub code search: eCFR 36 CFR Part 1191 Appendix B mirror github.com/lucas-amberg/ecfr-analyzer (public/ecfr/2025-02-06/36/XI/1191/Appendix B to Part 1191/Appendix B to Part 1191.md) and github.com/xerj-org/corpus-ada-2010/blob/main…. Web search summaries of access-board.gov/ada/chapter/ch02/ and corada.com/documents/2010ADAStandards/208-2 agree. The researcher's cited source github.com/usdoj-crt/beta-ada/blob/main/_page… ("One of six (or fraction of six) accessible parking spaces, but always at least one, must be van accessible"; "calculated separately for each parking facility") is consistent with the Standards. (ada.gov, access-board.gov, corada.com and web.archive.org were blocked by the sandbox proxy, so the .gov texts were verified through their official GitHub source repositories.)

research:market-and-stabilityAutodesk documents that there is no locking for concurrent data-shortcut edits in Autodesk Docs and that folder-structure changes may be lost.holds highThe claim is correct. Precise wording from Autodesk's Civil 3D help page "About Working on a Data Shortcut Project in Autodesk Docs" (section "Syncing modifications to data shortcuts"): "Currently, there is no safety mechanism such as locking to ensure that multiple users are not modifying data shortcuts concurrently. Changes to the data shortcut folder structure may be lost since they are not synced automatically. Note: Use the Refresh command before and after making changes to the folder structure to minimize the risk of losing changes." Two nuances worth stating: (1) the "may be lost" risk applies specifically to changes to the data-shortcut folder structure, which are written to disk and synced only on Refresh, switching projects, or closing Civil 3D, whereas creating/modifying individual shortcuts is written immediately and uploaded by Desktop Connector; drawing (DWG) files themselves ARE locked while open ("When a user opens a drawing, it is locked to other users"). (2) The statement is still present verbatim in the current Civil 3D 2027 edition of the page, where the product is renamed "Forma Data Management" (formerly Autodesk Docs / Autodesk Construction Cloud), so a current citation would read "Autodesk Docs / Forma Data Management".Primary (cited) source, retrieved verbatim through Autodesk's official Product Help search service because help.autodesk.com is blocked by this session's proxy: help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2023/ENU/?guid=G… — contains the exact sentences quoted in the correction. Independent confirmation 1: the Civil 3D 2027 edition of the same topic, "About Working on a Data Shortcut Project in Forma Data Management", help.autodesk.com/view/CIV3D/2027/ENU/?guid=G… — retains the identical "no safety mechanism such as locking ... may be lost since they are not synced automatically" text. Independent confirmation 2: Autodesk knowledge-base article "Data Shortcuts move to the old location from subfolders after the refresh in Civil 3D", autodesk.com/support/technical/article/caas/s… — lists "Multiple users change the Data Shortcuts location at the same time" as a cause and advises "Recreate the data shortcuts folder structure on one user account (so there are not simultaneous changes made by other users in the meantime)", noting the hierarchy lives in ShortcutsFolders.xml in the working folder. Supporting (desktop, not cloud-specific): Autodesk help "About the Data Shortcuts Editor", help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2025/ENU/Civil3D-… — "ensure that no one else is currently creating or editing the same set of data shortcuts ... Otherwise, one user can overwrite changes made by the other."
